\displaystyle\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H3}\end{pmatrix}\simeq\sum_{n\delta}\mathcal{Y}^{kl}_{n,\delta}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H4}\end{pmatrix}\simeq\sum_{n\delta j^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}\chi}\mathcal{Y}^{kl}_{n,\delta}\mathcal{F}^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}\chi}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H5}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\simeq\sum_{n\delta\chi}\mathcal{Y}^{kl}_{n,\delta}\mathcal{F}^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H8}\end{pmatrix}+\sum_{n\delta\chi}\mathcal{Y}^{kl}_{n,\delta}\mathcal{F}^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,(\beta\times f)\chi}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H9}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\simeq\sum_{n\delta\chi}\theta^{s(\delta)}_{\underline{\delta}}\theta^{s(\delta)}_{\underline{\delta^{\prime}}}Y^{kl}_{n,\delta}\theta^{s(\alpha)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\theta^{s(\delta)}_{\underline{\delta^{\prime}}}\theta^{s(\chi)}_{\underline{\chi}}\theta^{s(\beta)}_{\underline{\beta^{\prime}}}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}\theta^{s(\beta)}_{\underline{\beta}}\theta^{s(\beta)}_{\underline{\beta^{\prime}}}O^{ml,\beta}_{j}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H1}\end{pmatrix}+

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \sum_{n\delta\chi}\theta^{s(\delta)}_{\underline{\delta}}\theta^{s(\delta)}_{\underline{\delta^{\prime}}}Y^{kl}_{n,\delta}\theta^{s(\alpha)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\theta^{s(\delta)}_{\underline{\delta^{\prime}}}\theta^{s(\chi)}_{\underline{\chi}}\theta^{s(\beta\times f)}_{\underline{\beta^{\prime}}}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,(\beta\times f)\chi}\theta^{s(\beta)}_{\underline{\beta}}\theta^{s(\beta\times f)}_{\underline{\beta^{\prime}}}\theta^{s(\chi)}_{\underline{\chi}}\theta^{s(\chi\times f)}_{\underline{\chi}}\widetilde{O_{1}}^{ml,\beta(\beta\times f)\chi}_{jin,(\chi\times f)}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H1}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\simeq\sum_{n\delta\chi}\theta^{s(\beta)}_{\underline{\beta}}\theta^{s(\alpha)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\theta^{s(\delta)}_{\underline{\delta}}\theta^{s(\chi)}_{\underline{\chi}}Y^{kl}_{n,\delta}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}O^{ml,\beta}_{j}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H1}\end{pmatrix}+\sum_{n\delta\chi}\theta^{s(\beta)}_{\underline{\beta}}\theta^{s(\alpha)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\theta^{s(\delta)}_{\underline{\delta}}\theta^{s(\chi\times f)}_{\underline{\chi}}Y^{kl}_{n,\delta}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta(\chi\times f)}O^{ml,\beta}_{j}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H1f}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\simeq\sum_{n\chi\delta}2\theta^{s(\beta)}_{\underline{\beta}}\theta^{s(\alpha)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\theta^{s(\delta)}_{\underline{\delta}}\theta^{s(\chi)}_{\underline{\chi}}Y^{kl}_{n,\delta}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}O^{ml,\beta}_{j}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H1}\end{pmatrix},

(132)

where we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n\chi\delta}\widetilde{O_{1}}^{ml,\beta(\beta\times f)\chi}_{jin,(\chi\times f)}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H1}\end{pmatrix}\simeq\sum_{n\chi\delta}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H9}\end{pmatrix}=\sum_{n\chi\delta}\Xi^{im}_{lnj,\beta(\chi\times f)}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H8f}\end{pmatrix}\simeq\sum_{n\chi\delta}\Xi^{im}_{lnj,\beta(\chi\times f)}O^{ml,\beta}_{j}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{H1f}\end{pmatrix}.

(133)

Combining with the case that
\beta
is fermionic and the case
j
is m-type, the relation is written as

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}=n_{j}Y^{kl}_{n,\delta}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}O^{ml,\beta}_{j}.

(134)

With the ansatz Eq.(
110
) and Eq.(
111
), we have

\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}=\Phi^{ml,\beta}_{j}(\Phi^{kl,\delta}_{n})^{*}\sqrt{\frac{n_{j}n_{k}d_{m}d_{n}}{n_{m}n_{n}d_{j}d_{k}}}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}

(135)

Similar to
F
-move, we also require the dual
H
-move to be projective unitary:

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}\widetilde{H}^{kim^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}_{jln,\chi\delta}(\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta})^{*}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{m}}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(136)

If
m
is q-type, and in the fermion parity-even sector for example, this projective unitary condition for dual
H
-move can be viewed as the following projective map:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dh1}\end{pmatrix}+\frac{(\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl})^{*}}{2}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dh4fff}\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dh1}\end{pmatrix},

(137)

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}}{2}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dh1}\end{pmatrix}+\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dh4fff}\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dh4fff}\end{pmatrix},

(138)

In terms of matrix form, we have:

\widetilde{P}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{2}&\frac{(\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta)^{*}}_{jl}}{2}\\
\frac{\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}}{2}&\frac{1}{2}\end{array}\right),

(139)

which also satisfies
\widetilde{P}^{2}=\widetilde{P}
. Relation in Eq.(
134
) induces the following equivalence relation on
F
-move:

\displaystyle F^{imk,(\alpha\times f)\delta}_{lnj,(\beta\times f)\chi}=\frac{\widetilde{H}^{kim,(\alpha\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{jln,\chi\delta}O^{ml,\beta}_{j}}{\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}\mathcal{O}^{ml,(\beta\times f)}_{j}}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}=(\Lambda^{ml,\beta}_{j})^{*}\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type},

(140)

which is the equivalence between two
F
-moves with the fermion parity on the first two vertical fusion states changed. On the other hand, the projective unitary condition of dual
H
move also induce an additional condition for
F
-move:

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}\frac{d_{n}}{n_{n}}F^{im^{\prime}k,\alpha^{\prime}\delta}_{lnj,\beta^{\prime}\chi}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi})^{*}=\frac{d_{j}d_{k}n_{m}}{n_{j}n_{k}d_{m}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{m}}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(141)

where

\displaystyle\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}:=(\Lambda^{ml,\beta}_{j})^{*}\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}

(142)

is the combination of two phase factors, and it also satisfies
(\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl})^{*}=\Omega^{kim,(\alpha\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{jl}.

Inversely, if we sum over the states
\{m,\alpha,\beta\}
, the dual-
H
move also satisfies:

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}(\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}})^{*}\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{n}}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(143)

If
m
is q-type, and in the fermion parity-even sector for example, this projective unitary condition for dual
H
-move can be viewed as the following projective map:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dy1}\end{pmatrix}+\frac{(\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta})^{*}}{2}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dy4fff}\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dy1}\end{pmatrix},

(144)

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}}{2}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dy1}\end{pmatrix}+\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dy4fff}\end{pmatrix}\rightarrow\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{dy4fff}\end{pmatrix},

(145)

In terms of matrix form, we have:

\widetilde{P}^{\prime}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{2}&\frac{(\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta})^{*}}{2}\\
\frac{\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}}{2}&\frac{1}{2}\end{array}\right),

(146)

which also satisfies
(\widetilde{P}^{\prime})^{2}=\widetilde{P}^{\prime}
. Relation in Eq.(
134
) again induces the following equivalence relation on
F
-move:

\displaystyle(F^{imk,\alpha(\delta\times f)}_{lnj,\beta(\chi\times f)})^{*}=\frac{(\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)}Y^{kl}_{n,\delta})^{*}}{(\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}Y^{kl}_{n,(\delta\cdot f)})^{*}}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi})^{*}=(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{kl}_{n,\delta})^{*}\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi})^{*}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}.

(147)

which is the equivalence between two
F
-moves with the fermion parity on the second two vertical fusion states changed. We also have another condition for
F
-move:

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{m}}{n_{m}}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta^{\prime}}_{ln^{\prime}j,\beta\chi^{\prime}})^{*}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}=\frac{d_{j}d_{k}n_{n}}{n_{j}n_{k}d_{n}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{n}}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(148)

where

\displaystyle\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}:=(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{kl}_{n,\delta})^{*}\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta},

(149)

and it also satisfies
(\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta})^{*}=\Omega^{ki}_{jln,(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)}.

Similarly, we can also derive
H
-move from
F
,
Y
and
O
-moves:

\displaystyle H^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}=n_{i}Y^{kl}_{n,\delta}(F^{kmi,\alpha\chi}_{jnl,\beta\delta})^{*}O^{km,\alpha}_{i}.

(150)

When
m
is q-type, there is also such an equivalence relation:

\displaystyle H^{kim,(\alpha\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{jln,\chi\delta}={\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}H^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type},

(151)

and when
n
is q-type, we have another equivalence relation:

\displaystyle(H^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)})^{*}={\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}(H^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta})^{*}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}.

(152)

From Eq.(
134
) and Eq.(
150
), the phase factor between equivalent dual
H
-moves
{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}
is related to the phase factor between equivalent
H
-moves
\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}
by:

\displaystyle\zeta^{ikm,\alpha\beta}_{lj}=\Lambda^{im,\alpha}_{k}\Lambda^{ml,\beta}_{j}(\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl})^{*},

(153)

which can be proven from Eq.(
140
), Eq.(
147
) and Eq.(
150
). And
{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}=\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}
. The projectively-unitary conditions of
H
-moves are:

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}H^{kim^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}_{jln,\chi\delta}(H^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta})^{*}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{m}}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\zeta^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(154)

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}(H^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}})^{*}H^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{n}}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\zeta^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(155)

which will give exactly the same conditions for
F
-moves Eq.(
141
) and Eq.(
148
).

II.10
Relations among the phase factors

The four phase factors
\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kl}
,
\Xi^{ij}_{kln,\chi\delta}
,
\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}
and
\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}
are not independent. Consistency between the fermionic Pentagon equation in Eq.(
46
) and equivalence relatioins in Eq.(
34
), Eq.(
42
), Eq.(
140
) and Eq.(
147
) give rise to many relations among the phase factors. We will only show the following two relations here:

\displaystyle\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}=\Xi^{is}_{tkm,\eta\alpha}(\Xi^{stm,\eta\beta}_{lj})^{*},

(156)

\displaystyle\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}=\Xi^{si}_{jtn,\delta\eta}(\Xi^{sk}_{ltn,\chi\eta})^{*},

(157)

where strings
s,t
and fusion state
\eta
can be arbitrarily chosen as long as fusion rules are satisfied in the above two equations.

Now we show how to derive the first relation in Eq.(
156
). We divide the summation over
t
in Eq.(
46
) into two parts: the summation over
t
strings that are m-type, and the summation over
t
string sthat are q-type. Then we relabel
\eta
and
\psi
by
\eta\times f
and
\psi\times f
in the summation that
t
strings are q-type:

\displaystyle\underset{\epsilon}{\sum}F^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}F^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{\{\text{m-type }t\}\eta\psi\kappa}{\sum}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}F^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}F^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ +(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{\{\text{q-type }t\}(\eta\times f)(\psi\times f)(\kappa\times f)}{\sum}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,(\eta\times f)(\psi\times f)}F^{itn,(\psi\times f)\chi}_{lps,(\kappa\times f)\gamma}F^{jkt,(\eta\times f)(\kappa\times f)}_{lsq,\delta\phi}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{\{\text{m-type }t\}\eta\psi\kappa}{\sum}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}F^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}F^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ +(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{\{\text{q-type }t\}\eta\psi\kappa}{\sum}(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}\Omega^{nit,\psi\kappa}_{sl}\Xi^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{ls}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}F^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}F^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi},

(158)

where we note that for the summation over q-type
t
strings, we can only change the fermion parity for even number of fusion states for a single
F
-move (as the
F
-move should preserve fermion-parity), and only the state
\kappa
which is summed over can compensate the fermion-parity change in
F^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}
and
F^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}
. So that here
\kappa
must also be replaced by
\kappa\times f
. The summation
\underset{(\eta\times f)(\psi\times f)(\kappa\times f)}{\sum}
is actually equivalent to the summation
\underset{\eta\psi\kappa}{\sum}
(only up to changing the summation order). Comparing Eq.(
158
) with Eq.(
46
), we obtain

\displaystyle\underset{\{\text{q-type }t\}\eta\psi\kappa}{\sum}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}F^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}F^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi},=\underset{\{\text{q-type }t\}\eta\psi\kappa}{\sum}(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}\Omega^{nit,\psi\kappa}_{sl}\Xi^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{ls}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}F^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}F^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}.

(159)

We see that Eq.(
156
) is a simple solution to the above equation (up to a relabelling).

Then we derive Eq.(
157
). When string
s
is q-type, we relabel
\phi
and
\gamma
by
\phi\times f
and
\gamma\times f
. And in order to conserve the fermion-parity for a single
F
-move, we also need to replace
\kappa
by
\kappa\times f
:

\underset{\epsilon}{\sum}F^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,(\phi\times f)(\gamma\times f)}F^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{t\eta\psi\kappa}{\sum}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}F^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,(\kappa\times f)(\gamma\times f)}F^{jkt,\eta(\kappa\times f)}_{lsq,\delta(\phi\times f)}.

(160)

\underset{\epsilon}{\sum}(\Xi^{ij}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}F^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}F^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{t\eta\psi\kappa}{\sum}(\Xi^{it}_{lps,\kappa\gamma})^{*}(\Omega^{tj}_{qls,\kappa\phi})^{*}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}F^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}F^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}.

(161)

We see that Eq.(
157
) is a simple solution to the above equation (up to a relabelling).

Further, from Eq.(
142
) and Eq.(
156
), we have

\displaystyle(\Lambda^{tl,\kappa}_{s})^{*}\widetilde{\zeta}^{nit,\psi\kappa}_{sl}=(\Xi^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{ls})^{*}\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta\psi},

(162)

where strings
j,k
and fusion states
\eta
on right-hand side of the above equation can be chosen arbitrarily as long as fusion rules are satisfied. Here we can choose the values of
\Lambda^{ij,\alpha\beta}_{k}
and
\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}
arbitrarily as long as Eq.(
162
) is satisfied. And there always exists a gauge such that all
\Lambda^{ij,\alpha\beta}_{k}=1
. In such a gauge,
\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}
is determined by
\widetilde{\zeta}^{nit,\psi\kappa}_{sl}=(\Xi^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{ls})^{*}\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta\psi}
.

Also, Eq.(
129
) reduces to Eq.(
73
) if string
k
is identified with
i
and
l
is identified with
j
, which implies

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Lambda}^{ij}_{n,\chi\delta}=\widetilde{\zeta}^{ii}_{jjn,\chi\delta}.

(163)

Then from Eq.(
149
) and Eq.(
157
), we have

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{kl}_{n,\delta})^{*}\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}=\Xi^{si}_{jtn,\delta\eta}(\Xi^{sk}_{ltn,\chi\eta})^{*},

(164)

where strings
s,t
and fusion states
\eta
on right-hand side of the above equation can be chosen arbitrarily as long as fusion rules are satisfied. We note that if in certain example or under certain gauge we always have
\Xi^{si}_{jtn,\delta\eta}(\Xi^{sk}_{ltn,\chi\eta})^{*}=1
, we can then choose all
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{ij,\alpha\beta}_{k}=1
and all
\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}=1
.

II.11
Summary

We collect all conditions and list them below:

N^{ij}_{k}=B^{ij}_{k}+F^{ij}_{k}.

(165)

\underset{m}{\sum}\frac{N^{ij}_{m}N^{mk}_{l}}{n_{m}}=\underset{n}{\sum}\frac{N^{in}_{l}N^{jk}_{n}}{n_{n}}.

(166)

\underset{m}{\sum}\frac{B^{ij}_{m}F^{mk}_{l}+F^{ij}_{m}B^{mk}_{l}}{n_{m}}=\underset{n}{\sum}\frac{B^{in}_{l}F^{jk}_{n}+F^{in}_{l}B^{jk}_{n}}{n_{n}}.

(167)

F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}=0\text{ when }N^{ij}_{m}<1\text{ or }N^{mk}_{l}<1\text{ or }N^{jk}_{n}<1\text{ or }N^{in}_{l}<1\text{, or }s(\alpha)+s(\beta)+s(\chi)+s(\delta)=1\text{ mod }2.

(168)

\underset{ij}{\sum}(B^{ij}_{k})^{2}+(F^{ij}_{k})^{2}\geq 1.

(169)

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}F^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}_{kln,\chi\delta}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{m}}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kl}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(170)

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}})^{*}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{n}}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\Xi^{ij}_{kln,\chi\delta}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(171)

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}\frac{d_{n}}{n_{n}}F^{im^{\prime}k,\alpha^{\prime}\delta}_{lnj,\beta^{\prime}\chi}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi})^{*}=\frac{d_{j}d_{k}n_{m}}{n_{j}n_{k}d_{m}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{m}}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\Xi^{is}_{tkm,\eta\alpha}(\Xi^{stm,\eta\beta}_{lj})^{*}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(172)

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{m}}{n_{m}}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta^{\prime}}_{ln^{\prime}j,\beta\chi^{\prime}})^{*}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}=\frac{d_{j}d_{k}n_{n}}{n_{j}n_{k}d_{n}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{n}}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\Xi^{si}_{jtn,\delta\eta}(\Xi^{sk}_{ltn,\chi\eta})^{*}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(173)

(In Eq.(
172
) and Eq.(
173
), strings
s,t
and fusion state
\eta
can be arbitrarily chosen as long as fusion rules are satisfied.)

\displaystyle(\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kl})^{*}=\Xi^{ijm,(\alpha\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{kl},\ \ \ (\Xi^{ij}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}=\Xi^{ij}_{kln,(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)},

(174)

\underset{t\eta\phi\kappa}{\sum}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}F^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}F^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{\epsilon}{\sum}F^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}F^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}.

(175)

\underset{ij}{\sum}\frac{N^{ij}_{k}d_{i}d_{j}}{n_{i}n_{j}}=d_{k}D^{2},\text{ where }D^{2}=\underset{i}{\sum}\frac{d^{2}_{i}}{n_{i}}.

(176)

II.12
Hamiltonian for general 2D non-chiral topological orders

We construct the parent Hamiltonian that realizes the fixed-point wavefunctions satisfying all algebraic conditions listed in section
II.11
as the gapped ground state. The Hamiltonian is constructed on a 2D lattice, and let us consider a honeycomb lattice for example. It is a Hamiltonian that contains three terms:

\displaystyle\hat{H}=-\sum_{v}\hat{Q}_{v}-\sum_{e}\hat{D}_{l}-\sum_{p}\hat{B}_{p},

(177)

where
\sum_{v}
sums over all vertices,
\sum_{l}
sums over all links, and
\sum_{p}
sums over all plaquettes, as shown in Fig.
1
.

Figure 1
:

A honeycomb lattice. The vertices are labeled by
v
,
hexagons by
p
, and links by
l
.

The vertex term is defined in the same way as in Ref.
Gu et al. 2015
, which encodes all string fusion rules. Let the Hilbert space on a patch
G
be
V_{G}
. We expand the Hilbert space by adding an auxiliary qubit to each vertex
v
:

\displaystyle V^{ex}_{G}=V_{G}\otimes(\otimes_{\nu}V_{qubit}),

(178)

where
V_{qubit}
is a two dimensional Hilbert space of qubit
|I_{v}\rangle
,
I_{v}=0,1
. Then in the expanded Hilbert space
V^{ex}_{G}
,
\hat{Q}_{v}
acts on each vertex
v
and the three links connected to
v
as:

\displaystyle\hat{Q}_{v}\left|\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{ingoing}}}\right\rangle\otimes|I_{\alpha}\rangle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\left|\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{ingoing}}}\right\rangle\otimes|I_{\alpha}\rangle\text{, \ \ if }N^{ij}_{k}>0,I_{\alpha}=s(\alpha),\\
0\text{, \ \ otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

We see that
\hat{Q}_{v}
is a projector satisfying
\hat{Q}^{2}_{v}=\hat{Q}_{v}
. Equivalently, we can express
\hat{Q}_{v}
as

\displaystyle\hat{Q}_{v}=\sum_{ijk\alpha}\left|\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{ingoing}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle\left\langle\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{ingoing}}}\end{pmatrix}\right|,

(181)

where the states of fermionic ground state fixed-point wavefunctions
\Psi_{\text{fix}}
automatically satisfy
N^{ij}_{k}>0,I_{\alpha}=s(\alpha)
, and are assigned with ordered Majorana numbers on vertices.

The link term
\hat{D}_{l}
is needed when there are q-type strings involved.
\hat{D}_{l}
projects the following states into vacuum if the corresponding inner strings are q-type:

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{l}\begin{pmatrix}\left|\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dh4fff}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle-\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}\left|\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dh1}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle\end{pmatrix}=0\text{,\ \ if }m\text{ is q-type},

(182)

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{l}\begin{pmatrix}\left|\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dy4fff}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle-\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}\left|\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dy1}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle\end{pmatrix}=0\text{,\ \ if }n\text{ is q-type},

(183)

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{l}\begin{pmatrix}\left|\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{h3ff}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle-\zeta^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}\left|\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{H2}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle\end{pmatrix}=0\text{,\ \ if }m\text{ is q-type},

(184)

where the states if bosonic part ground state fixed-point wavefunction
\psi_{\text{fix}}
contain no Majorana number. Equivalently, we can express
\hat{D}_{l}
as

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{l}=

\displaystyle\underset{\{\text{q-type }m\}ijkl\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}(\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl})^{*}(-1)^{s(\beta)}\theta_{\underline{\alpha}}\theta_{\underline{\beta}}\left|\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dh4fff}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle+\left|\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dh1}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle\end{pmatrix}\left\langle\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dh1}}}\end{pmatrix}\right|

\displaystyle+\underset{\{\text{q-type }n\}ijkl\chi\delta}{\sum}\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}(\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta})^{*}(-1)^{s(\chi)}\theta_{\underline{\delta}}\theta_{\underline{\chi}}\left|\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dy4fff}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle+\left|\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dy1}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle\end{pmatrix}\left\langle\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dy1}}}\end{pmatrix}\right|

\displaystyle+\underset{\{\text{q-type }m\}ijkl\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}(\zeta^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl})^{*}(-1)^{s(\alpha)}\theta_{\underline{\beta}}\theta_{\underline{\alpha}}\left|\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{h3ff}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle+\left|\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{H2}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle\end{pmatrix}\left\langle\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{H2}}}\end{pmatrix}\right|,

(185)

where
\{\text{q-type }m\}
in the summation means that we only sum over
m
strings that are q-type. The attached Grassmann numbers for the first part of the
\hat{D}_{l}
operator for example is derived from the equivalence relation in Eq.(
127
):

\displaystyle\left|\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dh1}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle=(\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl})^{*}\theta^{s(\beta)}_{\underline{\beta}}\theta^{s(\alpha)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\theta^{s(\alpha\times f)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\theta^{s(\beta\times f)}_{\underline{\beta}}\left|\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dh4fff}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle=(\widetilde{\zeta}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl})^{*}(-1)^{s(\beta)}\theta_{\underline{\alpha}}\theta_{\underline{\beta}}\left|\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{dh4fff}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle.

(186)

And it is easy to see that
\hat{D}_{l}
as a projector satisfies
\hat{D}^{2}_{l}=\hat{D}_{l}
.

The plaquette term
\hat{B}_{p}
is also defined similarly as in Ref.
Gu et al. 2015
. It acts on the six vertices
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\lambda,\mu,\nu
and six inner links
a,b,c,d,e,f
of a hexagon
p
, and the six outer links
i,j,k,l,m,n
connected to the hexagon (the outer links are fixed). The Majorana number valued matrix element
\mathcal{B}^{a\alpha,b\beta,c\gamma,d\lambda,e\mu,f\nu}_{a^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime},b^{\prime}\beta^{\prime},c^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime},d^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime},e^{\prime}\mu^{\prime},f^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}(i,j,k,l,m,n)
is defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{a\alpha,b\beta,c\gamma,d\lambda,e\mu,f\nu}_{a^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime},b^{\prime}\beta^{\prime},c^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime},d^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime},e^{\prime}\mu^{\prime},f^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}(i,j,k,l,m,n)=\left\langle\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{B1}}}\end{pmatrix}\right|\hat{B}_{p}\left|\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{B2}}}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle.

(187)

The matrix
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{p}I\mathcal{U}_{p}
, where

\displaystyle(\mathcal{U}_{p})^{a\alpha,b\beta,c\gamma,d\lambda,e\mu,f\nu}_{trs,\chi\eta\varphi\epsilon}(i,j,k,l,m,n)=(\mathcal{H}^{jit,\chi\delta}_{fba,\alpha\beta})^{*}\mathcal{F}^{tfb,\delta\gamma}_{ckr,\kappa\eta}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{rfc,\kappa\lambda}_{dls,\rho\varphi}(\mathcal{F}^{fen,\nu^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{msd,\mu\rho})^{*}{\widetilde{\mathcal{O}_{2}}}^{fe,\nu\nu^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{nms,\epsilon},

(188)

where the involved
F
-moves,
H
-moves, dual
H
-moves and
\widetilde{O}
-moves should satisfy the equivalence relations in Eq.(
34
), Eq.(
42
), Eq.(
69
), Eq.(
128
), Eq.(
130
), Eq.(
151
) and Eq.(
152
) when certain strings are q-type.

Then we argue that our constructed Hamiltonian in Eq.(
177
) is a commuting-projector Hamiltonian. First, in Ref.
Gu et al. 2015
, it has been shown that
\hat{Q}_{v}
commutes with
\hat{B}_{p}
. Next, the link term
\hat{D}_{l}
automatically commutes with
\hat{Q}_{v}
as long as the states that
\hat{D}_{l}
projects onto satisfy all string fusion rules, which is exactly the case. Then the link term
\hat{D}_{l}
automatically commutes with
\hat{B}_{p}
as along as the involved
F
-moves,
H
-moves, dual
H
-moves and
\widetilde{O}
-moves satisfy their corresponding equivalence relations when certain strings are q-type, which is also the case.

III
Topological Invariant Partition Function

III.1
Partition function and spin structure

Figure 2
:
The graphical representation of the
G
-symbol is actually a
dual representation of the
F
-symbol.

Based on the above algebriac relations, we can construct the fellowing topological invariant partition function
Gu and Wen 2014
for an arbitrary triangulation of 3D spin manifold
\mathcal{M}
:

\mathcal{Z}=\frac{1}{D^{2N_{\nu}}}\sum_{ijklmn...\alpha\beta\chi\delta...}\prod_{\text{link}}\frac{d_{i}}{n_{i}}\int\prod_{\text{face}}d\theta^{s(\alpha)}_{\underline{\alpha}}d\overline{\theta}^{s(\alpha)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\prod_{E}(-1)^{s(\alpha)}\prod_{\text{tetrahedron}}(\mathcal{G}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{\sigma_{ijmkln}},

(189)

where
D^{2}=\sum_{i}d^{2}_{i}/n_{i}
is the total quantum dimension and
N_{\nu}
is the total number of vertices for a given triangulation. We evaluate the Grassmann integral on all interior faces, where we choose that
d\theta
always comes before
d\overline{\theta}
.

\mathcal{G}^{ijm}_{kln}
is the ordering-independent Grassmann valued
G
-symbol and
\sigma_{ijmkln}=\pm
is the orientation of the tetrahedron.:

(\mathcal{G}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{+}=\theta^{s(\alpha)}_{\underline{\alpha}}\theta^{s(\beta)}_{\underline{\beta}}\overline{\theta}^{s(\delta)}_{\underline{\delta}}\overline{\theta}^{s(\chi)}_{\underline{\chi}}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta},

(190)

(\mathcal{G}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{-}=\theta^{s(\chi)}_{\underline{\chi}}\theta^{s(\delta)}_{\underline{\delta}}\overline{\theta}^{s(\beta)}_{\underline{\beta}}\overline{\theta}^{s(\alpha)}_{\underline{\alpha}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}.

(191)

The
G
-symbol is actually the dual representation of the original
F
-symbol, as shown Fig.
2
, and
G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}
is related to
F
-symbol via:

G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}=\sqrt{\frac{n_{n}n_{m}}{d_{n}d_{m}}}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}.

(192)

Specifically,
\prod_{E}(-1)^{s(\alpha)}
is the spin structure term. We include this spin structure term such that the partition function is invariant under all Pachner moves
Gu and Wen 2014
, i.e. retriangulations. Mathematically, the fermionic partition function can only be defined on a spin manifold, i.e. a manifold that admits spin structures. It is known that an oriented manifold
\mathcal{M}
admits spin structures if and only if its second Stiefel-Whitney class
[\omega^{2}]\in H^{2}(\mathcal{M},\mathbb{Z}_{2})
vanishes. We denote the Poincare dual of
\omega^{2}
to be
\omega_{1}
in 2+1D, which is a set of some 1-simplices. Therefore, the requirement that
\omega^{2}
vanishes (being a coboundary) is equivalent to
\omega_{1}
being the boundary of some surface
E
:
\partial E=\omega_{1}
. Different choices of
E
correspond to different admitted choices of spin structures
\eta
, where
E
is the Poincare dual of the 1-cochain
\eta\in C^{1}(\mathcal{M},\mathbb{Z}_{2})
. In Ref.
Gu and Wen 2014
, the spin structure term is expressed as
\prod_{\omega_{1}}(-1)^{m(i)}
, where
m(i)
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
function defined on link
i
satisfying
s(\alpha)=m(i)+m(j)+m(k)
(mod 2). And
\omega_{1}
are certain links given as

\displaystyle\omega_{1}

\displaystyle=\text{
\lx@text@lbrace all 1-simplices\lx@text@rbrace+\lx@text@lbrace(02) in any $+$ tetrahedron (0123)\lx@text@rbrace+\lx@text@lbrace(13) in any $-$ tetrahedron (0123)\lx@text@rbrace}

\displaystyle=\text{
\lx@text@lbrace all 1-simplices\lx@text@rbrace+\lx@text@lbrace(02) in any 2-simplex\lx@text@rbrace+\lx@text@lbrace(03) in any 3-simplex\lx@text@rbrace},

(193)

where we have relabelled the vertices
A,B,C,D
in Fig.
2
by
0,1,2,3
, and the two expressions of
\omega_{1}
are equivalent as shown in Ref.
Tantivasadakarn 2017
. And both expressions are further equivalent to our spin structure term
\prod_{E}(-1)^{s(\alpha)}
.
It is known that all oriented 3D manifolds admit spin structures. The
E
surfaces for all eight time ordered 2-3 moves are listed in Ref.
Wang and Gu 2018
.

III.2
2-3 moves

In 2+1D, the first type of Pachner move is the 2-3 move. There are in total eight 2-3 moves that can be induced by a time ordering. The standard 2-3 move is given by

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \underset{\epsilon}{\sum}\int d\theta^{s(\epsilon)}_{\underline{\epsilon}}d\overline{\theta}^{s(\epsilon)}_{\underline{\epsilon}}(\mathcal{G}^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{-}(\mathcal{G}^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon})^{-}

\displaystyle\ \ =\underset{t\eta\phi\kappa}{\sum}\int d\theta^{s(\eta)}_{\underline{\eta}}d\overline{\theta}^{s(\eta)}_{\underline{\eta}}d\theta^{s(\phi)}_{\underline{\phi}}d\overline{\theta}^{s(\phi)}_{\underline{\phi}}d\theta^{s(\kappa)}_{\underline{\kappa}}d\overline{\theta}^{s(\kappa)}_{\underline{\kappa}}\frac{d_{t}}{n_{t}}(\mathcal{G}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\phi})^{-}(\mathcal{G}^{itn,\phi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma})^{-}(\mathcal{G}^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi})^{-},

(194)

where the spin structure term is trivial for the standrad 2-3 move, i.e.
\prod_{E}(-1)^{s(\alpha)}=1
. After integrating out the Grassmann numbers and comparing the rest Grassmann numbers on both sides, this equation is reduced to

\underset{\epsilon}{\sum}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{t\eta\psi\kappa}{\sum}\frac{d_{t}}{n_{t}}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}.

(195)

which is exactly the same as Eq.(
175
),
as shown graphically in Fig.
3
.

Figure 3
:
Graphical representation of the standard 2-3 move for
G
-symbol, or the fermionic Pentagon relation for
F
-symbol.

Figure 4
:
All possible time ordered 2-3 move for
G
-symbol.

The other seven 2-3 moves induced by time-ordering are:

\underset{\kappa}{\sum}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}=\underset{m\alpha\beta\epsilon}{\sum}(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{d_{m}}{n_{m}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon},

(196)

\underset{\beta}{\sum}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{s\phi\gamma\kappa}{\sum}\frac{d_{s}}{n_{s}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi},

(197)

\underset{\phi}{\sum}(G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{n\psi\beta\chi}{\sum}\frac{d_{n}}{n_{n}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}(G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma})^{*}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon},

(198)

\underset{\psi}{\sum}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}=\underset{q\epsilon\delta\phi}{\sum}(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{d_{q}}{n_{q}}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}(G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi})^{*},

(199)

\underset{\eta}{\sum}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{p\gamma\epsilon\chi}{\sum}\frac{d_{p}}{n_{p}}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}(G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma})^{*},

(200)

\underset{\chi}{\sum}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}(G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma})^{*}=\underset{j\alpha\eta\phi}{\sum}(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{d_{j}}{n_{j}}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}(G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*},

(201)

\underset{\gamma}{\sum}(G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}=\underset{k\beta\delta\eta}{\sum}(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{d_{k}}{n_{k}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}(G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi})^{*},

(202)

as shown graphically in Fig.
4
. Below we will show how to derive these seven 2-3 moves.

III.3
The additional relations among projective phase factors

Since the
G
-move and
F
-move are related through Eq.(
192
), we can rewrite the four projective unitary conditions in Eq.(
26
), Eq.(
36
), Eq.(
141
) and Eq.(
148
) in terms of
G
-move as

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}\frac{d_{n}d_{m}}{n_{n}n_{m}}G^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}_{kln,\chi\delta}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{m}}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kl}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(203)

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{n}d_{m}}{n_{n}n_{m}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{n}}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\Xi^{ij}_{kln,\chi\delta}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(204)

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}\frac{d_{n}d_{m}}{n_{n}n_{m}}G^{im^{\prime}k,\alpha^{\prime}\delta}_{lnj,\beta^{\prime}\chi}(G^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi})^{*}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{m}}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(205)

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{n}d_{m}}{n_{n}n_{m}}(G^{imk,\alpha\delta^{\prime}}_{ln^{\prime}j,\beta\chi^{\prime}})^{*}G^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{n_{n}}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(206)

Consistency between the fermionic Pentagon equation in Eq.(
195
) and four projective unitary conditions in Eq.(
203
)-Eq.(
206
) (all in terms of
G
-move) can induce many relations among the phase factors. Here we only focus on the relations that are required to fully construct the fermionic partition function in Eq.(
189
).

The above four projective unitary conditions induce the following four equivalence relations for
G
-move:

G^{ijm,(\alpha\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{kln,\chi\delta}=\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kl}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type}

(207)

(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)})^{*}=\Xi^{ij}_{kln,\chi\delta}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}

(208)

G^{imk,(\alpha\times f)\delta}_{lnj,(\beta\times f)\chi}=\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}G^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type}

(209)

(G^{imk,\alpha(\delta\times f)}_{lnj,\beta(\chi\times f)})^{*}=\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}(G^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi})^{*}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}

(210)

In addition to the four phase factors
\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kl}
,
\Xi^{ij}_{kln,\chi\delta}
,
\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}
and
\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}
we defined above, we need to define a new phase factor
\Delta^{mji,\alpha\delta}_{nl}
to construct the topological invariant partition function, as the following

G^{ijm,(\alpha\times f)\beta}_{kln,\chi(\delta\times f)}=\Delta^{mji,\alpha\delta}_{nl}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ if }i\text{ is q-type,}

(211)

which corresponds to the changing of fermion parity on two diagonal fusion states
\alpha
and
\delta
, and the phase factor
\Delta^{mji,\alpha\delta}_{nl}
can be explicitly constructed through a sequence of
F
-move and
O
-moves, as introduced in Appendix
C
.

To derive the rest seven 2-3 moves induced by time-ordering, i.e., to fully establish the topological invariance of partition function, the following four relations on phase factors are required (See full details in Appendix
D
):

\displaystyle\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta\psi}=\Omega^{nit,\psi\kappa}_{sl}\Xi^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{ls},

(212)

\displaystyle\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qp}=\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kn}(\Delta^{nkm,\beta\epsilon}_{qp})^{*},

(213)

\displaystyle(\Xi^{it}_{lps,\kappa\gamma})^{*}=(\Xi^{ij}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}\Omega^{tj}_{qls,\kappa\phi},

(214)

\displaystyle(\Omega^{mi}_{sqp,\epsilon\gamma})^{*}=\Omega^{nm}_{qlp,\chi\epsilon}(\Omega^{ni}_{slp,\chi\gamma})^{*},

(215)

All above relations can be obtained as simple solutions by comparing the fermionic Pentagon equation in Eq.(
195
) with the equivalence relations in Eq.(
207
)-Eq.(
211
). Eq. (
212
) and Eq. (
214
) are derived in section
II.10
. We can obtain the rest relations in Eq.(
213
) and Eq.(
215
) in a similar manner.

III.4
1-4 moves

The second type of Pachner move is the 1-4 move. There are three different 1-4 moves induced by a global time ordering:

G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{1}{D^{2}}\underset{ntkl}{\sum}\underset{\beta\eta\psi\chi\kappa\delta}{\sum}\frac{d_{n}d_{t}d_{k}d_{l}}{n_{n}n_{t}n_{k}n_{l}}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}(G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon})^{*}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi},

(216)

G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{1}{D^{2}}\underset{ijts}{\sum}\underset{\alpha\eta\psi\phi\kappa\gamma}{\sum}\frac{d_{i}d_{j}d_{t}d_{s}}{n_{i}n_{j}n_{t}n_{s}}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}(G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi},

(217)

G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{1}{D^{2}}\underset{mjkq}{\sum}\underset{\alpha\beta\eta\epsilon\phi\delta}{\sum}\frac{d_{m}d_{j}d_{k}d_{q}}{n_{m}n_{j}n_{k}n_{q}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}(G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi})^{*}.

(218)

Combining all the 2-3 moves in Eq.(
195
)-Eq.(
202
) and all the 1-4 moves in Eq.(
216
)-Eq.(
218
), the following relations can be derived:

\sum_{kjm\alpha\beta\chi}\frac{d_{k}d_{j}d_{m}}{n_{k}n_{j}n_{m}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}=D^{2},

(219)

\sum_{ijn\alpha\chi\delta}\frac{d_{i}d_{j}d_{n}}{n_{i}n_{j}n_{n}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}=D^{2},

(220)

\sum_{ilm\alpha\beta\delta}\frac{d_{i}d_{l}d_{m}}{n_{i}n_{l}n_{m}}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}=D^{2},

(221)

\sum_{kln\beta\chi\delta}\frac{d_{k}d_{l}d_{n}}{n_{k}n_{l}n_{n}}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}=D^{2},

(222)

For example, we show how to derive Eq.(
219
) by comparing Eq.(
195
) with Eq.(
216
). We multiply by
(G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon})^{*}
and sum over
l,k,n,\beta,\chi,\delta
on both sides of Eq.(
195
):

\underset{\epsilon,lkn\beta\chi\delta}{\sum}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}(G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon})^{*}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{t\eta\psi\kappa,lkn\beta\chi\delta}{\sum}\frac{d_{t}}{n_{t}}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}(G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon})^{*}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}.

(223)

We see that the difference between Eq.(
195
) and Eq.(
216
) can be exactly compensated by Eq.(
219
) up to a relabelling on indices.

Eq.(
219
)-Eq.(
222
) together with the projective unitary conditions in Eq.(
203
)-Eq.(
206
) further imply:

\underset{ij}{\sum}\frac{N^{ij}_{k}d_{i}d_{j}}{n_{i}n_{j}}=d_{k}D^{2}.

(224)

which is exactly Eq.(
176
). For example, we show how to derive Eq.(
224
) from Eq.(
219
) and Eq.(
204
) in two cases below:

(1) If
n
is m-type, replacing

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{m}d_{n}}{n_{m}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}=1

into Eq.(
219
), we obtain

\sum_{kj\chi}\frac{d_{k}d_{j}}{n_{k}n_{j}d_{n}}=D^{2},

(225)

where
\sum_{\chi}
only counts the number of possible states of
\chi
and can be replaced by
N^{jk}_{n}
. We find it is exactly Eq.(
224
) up to a relabelling on indices.

(2) If
n
is q-type, replacing

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{m}d_{n}}{n_{m}n_{n}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}=\frac{1}{n_{n}}

into Eq.(
219
), it becomes the same equation as above. So that we can again obtain Eq.(
224
).

IV
Examples

In this section, we derive all equivalence relations and
F
-moves for all following examples, as listed in Appendix
B.4
. We write down the explicit expressions of the phase factors
\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kl}
,
\Xi^{ij}_{kln,\chi\delta}
,
\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}=\Xi^{is}_{tkm,\eta\alpha}(\Xi^{stm,\eta\beta}_{lj})^{*}
and
\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}=\Xi^{si}_{jtn,\delta\eta}(\Xi^{sk}_{ltn,\chi\eta})^{*}
in Appendix
B.5
. We note that we didn’t choose the gauge such that all
\Lambda^{ij,\alpha\beta}_{k}=1
as illustrated in section
II.10
. But we choose the gauge such that all
\Xi^{si}_{jtn,\delta\eta}(\Xi^{sk}_{ltn,\chi\eta})^{*}=1
, and then all
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{ij,\alpha\beta}_{k}=1
and all
\widetilde{\zeta}^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}=1
. We verify that all
F
-moves in each example exactly satisfy the corresponding four projective unitary condition, as well as the fermionic Pentagon equation.

IV.1
Fermionic topological order
SO(3)_{6}/\psi

In the fermionic topological phase
SO(3)_{6}/\psi
, we have two strings
\{1,s\}
, where
1
is the vacuum string, and
s
is an m-type string. The quantum dimensions are given by

\displaystyle d_{1}=1,\ \ \ d_{s}=1+\sqrt{2}.

(226)

The fusion rules are given by

\displaystyle 1\times s=s\times 1=s,\ \ \ s\times s=1+\mathbb{C}^{1|1}s,

(227)

where we use the notation
\mathbb{C}^{B^{ab}_{c}|F^{ab}_{c}}
to denote the number of bosonic and fermionic fusion state for a given fusion space
V^{ab}_{c}
. The fusion rules written as fusion tensors are

B^{11}_{1}=B^{1s}_{s}=B^{s1}_{s}=B^{ss}_{1}=B^{ss}_{s}=F^{ss}_{s}=1,

(228)

and all other fusion tensors are zero.

Since the fermionic theory
SO(3)_{6}/\psi
only contains m-type strings, and the dimension of endomorphism for m-type strings
n_{i}=1
.
We list all
F
-moves of
SO(3)_{6}/\psi
in Appendix
B.6
.

IV.2
Majorana toric code

In the Majorana toric code, we have two string types
\{1,\sigma\}
, where
1
is the vacuum string,
\sigma
is a q-type Majorana string.

The quantum dimensions are given by

\displaystyle d_{1}=1,\ \ \ d_{\sigma}=\sqrt{2}.

(229)

The fusion rules are given by

\displaystyle 1\times\sigma=\sigma\times 1=\mathbb{C}^{1|1}\sigma,\ \ \ \sigma\times\sigma=\mathbb{C}^{1|1}1,

(230)

written in fusion tensors as

B^{11}_{1}=B^{1\sigma}_{\sigma}=B^{\sigma 1}_{\sigma}=B^{\sigma\sigma}_{1}=F^{1\sigma}_{\sigma}=F^{\sigma 1}_{\sigma}=F^{\sigma\sigma}_{1}=1,

(231)

and all other fusion tensors are zero.

The four projective unitary conditions for Majorana toric code are:

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}F^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}_{kln,\chi\delta}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(232)

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}})^{*}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\Theta_{fi}^{\Gamma_{\delta}}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

\text{ \\
where }\Theta_{fi}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}1\text{, \ \ if }i\text{ is m-type
}\\
i\text{, \ \ if }i\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.\text{\ \ and }\Gamma_{\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}1\text{, \ \ if }s(\delta)=0\\
\text{, \ \ if }s(\delta)=1\end{array}\right.

(233)

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}\frac{d_{n}}{n_{n}}F^{im^{\prime}k,\alpha^{\prime}\delta}_{lnj,\beta^{\prime}\chi}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi})^{*}=\frac{d_{j}d_{k}n_{m}}{n_{j}n_{k}d_{m}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\Theta_{fi}^{\Gamma_{\alpha}}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{m}}{n_{m}}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta^{\prime}}_{ln^{\prime}j,\beta\chi^{\prime}})^{*}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}=\frac{d_{j}d_{k}n_{n}}{n_{j}n_{k}d_{n}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(234)

We list all
F
-moves of Majorana toric code in Appendix
B.7.2
. We have checked numerically that the
F
-moves satisfy all above projective unitary conditions.

IV.3
Fermionic topological order
\frac{1}{2}E_{6}/\psi

In the fermionic topological phase
\frac{1}{2}E_{6}/\psi
, we have two string types
\{1,x\}
, where
1
is the vacuum string, and
x
is a q-type Majorana string.

The quantum dimensions are given by

\displaystyle d_{1}=1,\ \ \ d_{x}=1+\sqrt{3}.

(235)

The fusion rules are given by

\displaystyle 1\times x=x\times 1=\mathbb{C}^{1|1}x,\ \ \ x\times x=\mathbb{C}^{1|1}1+\mathbb{C}^{2|2}x,

(236)

written in fusion tensors as

\displaystyle B^{11}_{1}=B^{1x}_{x}=B^{x1}_{x}=B^{xx}_{1}=F^{1x}_{x}=F^{x1}_{x}=F^{xx}_{1}=1,\ \ \ \ \ \ B^{xx}_{x}=F^{xx}_{x}=2,

(237)

where all other fusion tensors vanish. We note that non-trivially
B^{xx}_{x}=2
, i.e. we have two bosonic fusion states if we fuse two
x
and again obtain
x
. We denote the two bosonic fusion states as ”1” and ”2” respectively.

By fermion condensation, invoking the
F
-symbols in the bosonic
\frac{1}{2}E_{6}
theory in a certain gauge in Eq.(
341
)-Eq.(
346
) in Appendix
B
, the equivalence relations Eq.(
287
), Eq.(
291
), Eq.(
293
), Eq.(
297
) and Eq.(
300
) have the forms:

F^{ijm,(\alpha\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{kln,\chi\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type and }B^{mk}_{l}=1\\
(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type and }B^{mk}_{l}=2\end{array}\right.

(238)

(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)})^{*}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Theta_{fi}^{\Gamma_{\delta}}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type and }B^{in}_{l}=1\\
(\sigma_{x})_{B(\delta\times f)B(\delta)}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type and }B^{in}_{l}=2\end{array}\right.

(239)

O^{ij,(\alpha\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{k}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}O^{ij,\alpha\beta}_{k}\text{, \ \ if }k\text{ is q-type and }B^{ij}_{k}=1\\
(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\alpha\times f)B(\alpha)}(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}O^{ij,\alpha\beta}_{k}\text{, \ \ if }k\text{ is q-type and }B^{ij}_{k}=2\end{array}\right.

(240)

Our notation of Pauli matrices appear whenever a changing of fermion-parity alters the bosonic states in any equivalence relation. If a Pauli matrix
\sigma_{i}
, where
i=x,y
, corresponds to a fusion state
\alpha
, then the rows represent
B(\alpha\times f)
is 1 or 2, and columns of
\sigma_{i}
represent the values of
B(\alpha)
is 1 or 2. And we use
(\sigma_{i})_{B(\alpha\times f)B(\alpha)}
to represent the entry of
\sigma_{i}
in row
B(\alpha\times f)
and column
B(\alpha)
, which is simply a phase factor. For example, the notation
(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}
in Eq.(
238
) represents the phase factor:

(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}i\text{, \ \ if }B(\beta\times f)=1,B(\beta)=2\\
-i\text{, \ \ if }B(\beta\times f)=2,B(\beta)=1\\
0\text{, \ \ otherwise}\end{array}\right.

(241)

And the notation
(\sigma_{x})_{B(\delta\times f)B(\delta)}
in Eq.(
239
) represents:

(\sigma_{x})_{B(\delta\times f)B(\delta)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}1\text{, \ \ if }B(\delta\times f)=1,B(\delta)=2\\
1\text{, \ \ if }B(\delta\times f)=2,B(\delta)=1\\
0\text{, \ \ otherwise}\end{array}\right.

(242)

Taking the gauge choice Eq.(
77
) on the involved
O
-move in Eq.(
134
),

O^{ml,(\beta\times f)}_{j}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}O^{ml,\beta}_{j}\text{, \ \ if }j\text{ is q-type and }B^{ml}_{j}=1\\
(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}O^{ml,\beta}_{j}\text{, \ \ if }j\text{ is q-type and }B^{ml}_{j}=2\end{array}\right.

(243)

\widetilde{H}^{kim,(\alpha\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{jln,\chi\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Theta_{fi}^{\Gamma_{\alpha}}\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}\text{,\ \ if }m\text{ is q-type, }B^{im}_{k}=1,B^{ml}_{j}=1\\
\Theta_{fi}^{\Gamma_{\alpha}}(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}\text{,\ \ if }m\text{ is q-type, }B^{im}_{k}=1,B^{ml}_{j}=2\\
(\sigma_{x})_{B(\alpha\times f)B(\alpha)}\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}\text{,\ \ if }m\text{ is q-type, }B^{im}_{k}=2,B^{ml}_{j}=1\\
(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}(\sigma_{x})_{B(\alpha\times f)B(\alpha)}\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta}\text{,\ \ if }m\text{ is q-type, }B^{im}_{k}=2,B^{ml}_{j}=2\end{array}\right.

(244)

(\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)})^{*}=(\widetilde{H}^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jln,\chi\delta})^{*}\text{,\ \ if }n\text{ is q-type, }.

(245)

By Eq.(
134
), we replace the dual
H
-move in Eq.(
244
) by
F
-move, which will also bring a phase factor from equivalent
O
-moves in Eq.(
243
). We should also note that when two such matrices multiply together, we are not doing matrix multiplication, but we should multiply by each entry. For example,
(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}=(-\sigma_{x})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}
.

We obtain the four projective unitary conditions for fermonic topological order
\frac{1}{2}E_{6}/\psi
:

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}F^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}_{kln,\chi\delta}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type and }B^{mk}_{l}=1\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+(-\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type and }B^{mk}_{l}=2\end{array}\right.

(246)

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}})^{*}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\Theta_{fi}^{\Gamma_{\delta}}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type and }B^{in}_{l}=1\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+(\sigma_{x})_{B(\delta\times f)B(\delta)}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type and }B^{in}_{l}=2\end{array}\right.

(247)

\text{ \\
where }\Theta_{fi}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}1\text{, \ \ if }i\text{ is m-type
}\\
i\text{, \ \ if }i\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.\text{ and }\Gamma_{\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}1\text{, \ \ if }s(\delta)=0\\
\text{, \ \ if }s(\delta)=1\end{array}\right.,

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}\frac{d_{n}}{n_{n}}F^{im^{\prime}k,\alpha^{\prime}\delta}_{lnj,\beta^{\prime}\chi}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi})^{*}=\frac{d_{j}d_{k}n_{m}}{n_{j}n_{k}d_{m}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\Theta_{fi}^{\Gamma_{\alpha}}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}}),\\
\text{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type, }B^{im}_{k}=1,B^{ml}_{j}=1\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\Theta_{fi}^{\Gamma_{\alpha}}(\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}}),\\
\text{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type, }B^{im}_{k}=1,B^{ml}_{j}=2\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+(\sigma_{x})_{B(\alpha\times f)B(\alpha)}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}}),\\
\text{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type, }B^{im}_{k}=2,B^{ml}_{j}=1\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+(\sigma_{y})_{B(\beta\times f)B(\beta)}(\sigma_{x})_{B(\alpha\times f)B(\alpha)}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}}),\\
\text{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type, }B^{im}_{k}=2,B^{ml}_{j}=2\end{array}\right.

(248)

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{m}}{n_{m}}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta^{\prime}}_{ln^{\prime}j,\beta\chi^{\prime}})^{*}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}=\frac{d_{j}d_{k}n_{n}}{n_{j}n_{k}d_{n}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(249)

We list all
F
-moves of
\frac{1}{2}E_{6}/\psi
in Appendix
B.8.2
. We have checked numerically that the
F
-moves satisfies all above projective unitary conditions.

IV.4
Fermionic topological order
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}/\psi_{N}

\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}/\psi_{N}
is the Tambara-Yamagami category after condensing the fermion
\psi_{N}
, with symmetric non-degenerate bicharacter of type
t
(
1\leq t\leq 2N-1
and
\operatorname{gcd}(t,2N)=1
):

\displaystyle\chi_{t}(a,b)=e^{2\pi itab/(2N)},

(250)

and
\varkappa
is the Frobenius-Schur indicator. The fusion category
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}/\psi_{N}
is a generalization of the Majorana toric code, i.e., the Majorana toric code is the special case
N=1
in
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}/\psi_{N}
.

\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}/\psi_{N}
has string types
\mathbb{Z}_{N}\cup\{\sigma\}
, where
\sigma
is a q-type string,
\mathbb{Z}_{N}=\{0,1,\cdots,N-1\}
are labels of m-type strings.

The quantum dimensions are given by

\displaystyle d_{i}=1,\ \ \ d_{\sigma}=\sqrt{2N}.

(251)

The fusion rules are given by

\displaystyle a\times b=\mathbb{C}^{(1-\left\lfloor\frac{a+b}{N}\right\rfloor)|\left\lfloor\frac{a+b}{N}\right\rfloor}[a+b]_{N},\ \ \ a\times\sigma=\sigma\times a=\mathbb{C}^{1|1}\sigma,\ \ \ \sigma\times\sigma=\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}}\mathbb{C}^{1|1}a,

(252)

where we define
[a+b]_{N}=(a+b)
(mod
N
), and
\left\lfloor\frac{a+b}{N}\right\rfloor
means taking the integer part of
\frac{a+b}{N}
. In fusion tensors,

\displaystyle B^{ab}_{[a+b]_{N}}=1,F^{ab}_{[a+b]_{N}}=0,\text{if }a+b<N,

\displaystyle B^{ab}_{[a+b]_{N}}=0,F^{ab}_{[a+b]_{N}}=1,\text{if }a+b\geq N,

\displaystyle B^{1\sigma}_{\sigma}=B^{\sigma 1}_{\sigma}=B^{\sigma\sigma}_{1}=F^{1\sigma}_{\sigma}=F^{\sigma 1}_{\sigma}=F^{\sigma\sigma}_{1}=1,

(253)

where all other fusion tensors vanish.

We only consider
N
to be odd here. The four projective unitary conditions for
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}/\psi_{N}
are:

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}F^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}_{kln,\chi\delta}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m,k\text{ are q-type, and }l\text{ is m-type}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+(-1)^{k}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }m,l\text{ are q-type, and }k\text{ is m-type}\end{array}\right.

(254)

where we note that in the third case,
m,l\text{ are q-type and }k\text{ is m-type}
, which means that
m,l
are the
\sigma
string and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}
. So that In the phase factor
(-1)^{k}
,
k
just takes value in
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
.

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}})^{*}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+(-1)^{i}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n,l\text{ are q-type, and }i\text{ is m-type}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+(-1)^{l}\Theta_{fi}^{\Gamma_{\delta}}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n,i\text{ are q-type, and }l\text{ is m-type}\end{array}\right.

\text{ \\
where }\Theta_{fi}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}1\text{, \ \ if }i\text{ is m-type
}\\
i\text{, \ \ if }i\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.\text{\ \ and }\Gamma_{\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}1\text{, \ \ if }s(\delta)=0\\
\text{, \ \ if }s(\delta)=1\end{array}\right.

(255)

where similarly in the second case
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}
, while in the third case
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}
.

\underset{n\chi\delta}{\sum}\frac{d_{n}}{n_{n}}F^{im^{\prime}k,\alpha^{\prime}\delta}_{lnj,\beta^{\prime}\chi}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi})^{*}=\frac{d_{j}d_{k}n_{m}}{n_{j}n_{k}d_{m}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+(-1)^{i}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}}),\\
\text{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ if }m,k,l\text{ are q-type, and }i,j\text{ are m-type}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+(-1)^{i+l}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}}),\\
\text{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ if }m,j,k\text{ are q-type, and }i,l\text{ are m-type}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+(-1)^{k}\Theta_{fi}^{\Gamma_{\alpha}}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}}),\\
\text{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ if }m,i,l\text{ are q-type, and }j,k\text{ are m-type}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}+(-1)^{k+l}\Theta_{fi}^{\Gamma_{\alpha}}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{(\alpha\times f)\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{(\beta\times f)\beta^{\prime}}),\\
\text{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ if }m,i,j\text{ are q-type, and }k,l\text{ are m-type}\end{array}\right.

(256)

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{m}}{n_{m}}(F^{imk,\alpha\delta^{\prime}}_{ln^{\prime}j,\beta\chi^{\prime}})^{*}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}=\frac{d_{j}d_{k}n_{n}}{n_{j}n_{k}d_{n}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is m-type
}\\
\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{\chi\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{\delta\delta^{\prime}}+\delta_{nn^{\prime}}\delta_{(\chi\times f)\chi^{\prime}}\delta_{(\delta\times f)\delta^{\prime}})\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type}\end{array}\right.

(257)

We list all
F
-moves of
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}/\psi_{N}
in Appendix
B.9.2
.

V
Conclusion and discussions

In conclusion, we obtain a hopefully complete classification of all 2D non-chiral fermionic topological orders characterized by a set of tensors
(N^{ij}_{k},F^{ij}_{k},F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta},n_{i},d_{i})
, which satisfy a set of nonlinear algebraic equations parameterized by phase factors
\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kl}
,
\Xi^{ij}_{kln,\chi\delta}
,
\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}
and
\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}
.
By considering the consistency between the fermionic Pentagon equation and the four projective unitary conditions, we get more relations for the phase factors
\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kl}
,
\Xi^{ij}_{kln,\chi\delta}
,
\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}
,
\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}
,
\Delta^{mji,\alpha\delta}_{nl}
, from which we can define a topological invariant partition function for arbitrary 3-manifold with a spin structure. Finally, we also discussed four examples which satisfy all algebraic conditions.

For future study, it would also be very interesting to generalize the construction in Ref.
Cheng et al. 2017
and Ref.
Heinrich et al. 2016
for 2D non-chiral fermionic symmetry-enriched topological (fSET) phases, including those anomalous 2D fermionic SET states
Wang et al. 2013
;
Bonderson et al. 2013
;
Chen et al. 2014
;
Metlitski et al. 2015
;
Tata et al. 2021
;
Bulmash and Barkeshli 2022a
which can only exist on the surface of some 3D fermionic symmetry-protected (fSPT) phases. We believe that the q-type strings, or called Majorana-type strings, are very likely to characterize the anomaly of 3D fSPT phases with Kitaev-chain decoration
Wang and Gu 2020
. Moreover, it will also be very interesting to understand the generic algebraic structure
Lan et al. 2017
;
Aasen et al. 2021
;
Bulmash and Barkeshli 2022b
of fSET phases from equivalence class of symmetric fLU transformations.

VI
acknowledgements

We are grateful to Tian Lan for enlightening discussions. We also thank Zhenghan Wang for insightful discussions on the
\frac{1}{2}E_{6}
example. This work was supported by Research Grant Council of Hong Kong(GRF 14306420,
ANR/RGC Joint Research Scheme no. A-CUHK402/18).

Appendix A
Super Fusion Category

This section is a review on some basic concepts about super fusion categories
Brundan and Ellis 2017
;
Usher 2018
;
Aasen et al. 2019
. In the point of view of category theory, the string types are the simple objects in a super fusion category, or more precisely, a super pivotal category
\mathcal{S}
, where the ”pivotal” structure is covered by the
H
-move we defined in the fermionic string-net model. The super pivotal category only covers 2D fermoinic topological orders that can be obtained from fermion condensation. We believe that our approach from fixed-point wavefunction realizes more general fermonic topological than super pivotal category.

The number
n_{i}
we introduced in the general fermionic string-net model is actually the dimension of endomorphism of the string:

n_{i}:=\text{dim End}(i).

(258)

Explicitly, the string types are further divided into m-type strings and q-type strings:

(1) A string
i
is an m-type string if

\text{End}(i)\cong\mathbb{C},

(259)

where End
(i)
is the endomorphism algebra of string
i
(maps from string
i
to itself), and the dimension of it is dim(End
(i))=1
. It means that the map from an m-type string to itself is one dimensional.

(2) A string
i
is a q-type string if

\text{End}(i)\cong\mathbb{C}l_{1},

(260)

where dim End
(i)=2
. The first complex Clifford algebra is
\mathbb{C}l_{1}=\{1,\gamma\}
, where
1
is the parity-even generator and
\gamma
is the parity-odd generator.

A.1
The modified fusion space

In fermionic case, the fusion space
V^{ij}_{k}
is different from the super vector space
\Delta^{ij}_{k}
appearing in the fusion rule (while in bosonic case,
V^{ij}_{k}\cong\Delta^{ij}_{k}
):

(1) The super vector space
\Delta^{ij}_{k}
is defined as the space of fusion coefficients in the string fusion rule:

i\otimes j\cong\underset{k}{\oplus}\Delta^{ij}_{k}\cdot k,

(261)

where the fusion outcome is generally a composite object, which can be written in the from of multiplying an object with a super vector space.

(2) The fusion space
V^{ij}_{k}
is defined as the vector space of morphisms from
k
to
i\otimes j
:

V^{ij}_{k}:=\text{mor}(k\rightarrow i\otimes j)\cong\Delta^{ij}_{k}\otimes\text{mor}(k\rightarrow k)\cong\Delta^{ij}_{k}\otimes\text{End}(k),

(262)

and therefore the dimension of the super vector space
\Delta^{ij}_{k}
is given by

\eta^{ij}_{k}:=\text{dim}(\Delta^{ij}_{k})=\frac{N^{ij}_{k}}{\text{dim End}(k)},

(263)

where
N^{ij}_{k}=
dim
(V^{ij}_{k})
is the dimension of the fusion space.

A.2
The modified tensor product

In larger fusion spaces involving more strings, the tensor product should also be modified. For example, we consider the fusion space
V^{ijk}_{l}
in Fig.
5
. In bosonic case, we have

V^{ijk}_{l}\cong\underset{m}{\oplus}V^{ij}_{m}\otimes V^{mk}_{l}.

(264)

Figure 5
:
The fusion space of fusing three strings
i,j,k
into
l
.

However, in fermionic case, the two fusion spaces on two sides are not isomorphic. Explicitly,

\displaystyle V^{ijk}_{l}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\text{mor}(l\rightarrow i\otimes j\otimes k)

(265)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{m}{\oplus}\Delta^{ij}_{m}\otimes\text{mor}(l\rightarrow m\otimes k)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{m}{\oplus}\Delta^{ij}_{m}\otimes\Delta^{mk}_{l}\otimes\text{End}(l),

and

\displaystyle\underset{m}{\oplus}V^{ij}_{m}\otimes V^{mk}_{l}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{m}{\oplus}(\Delta^{ij}_{m}\otimes\text{End}(m))\otimes(\Delta^{mk}_{l}\otimes\text{End}(l))

(266)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{m}{\oplus}\Delta^{ij}_{m}\otimes\Delta^{mk}_{l}\otimes\text{End}(m)\otimes\text{End}(l).

Therefore in fermionic case the fusion space
V^{ijk}_{l}
should be decomposed as

V^{ijk}_{l}\cong\underset{m}{\oplus}V^{ij}_{m}\otimes_{\text{End}(m)}V^{mk}_{l}:=\underset{m}{\oplus}V^{ij}_{m}\otimes V^{mk}_{l}\backslash\text{End}(m),

(267)

where
\otimes_{\text{End}(m)}
is the relative tensor product, which is just the original tensor product modulo out the equivalence relations induced by End
(m)
.

The support dimension of
V^{ijk}_{l}
is

\text{dim}(V^{ijk}_{l})=\underset{m}{\sum}\frac{N^{ij}_{m}N^{mk}_{l}}{\text{dim End}(m)}.

(268)

A.3
F
-move

We define the
F
-move as

F^{ijk}_{l}:\underset{n}{\oplus}V^{in}_{l}\otimes_{\text{End}(n)}V^{jk}_{n}\rightarrow\underset{m}{\oplus}V^{ij}_{m}\otimes_{\text{End}(m)}V^{mk}_{l},

(269)

where the support dimensions on two sides are equal:

\underset{m}{\sum}\frac{N^{ij}_{m}N^{mk}_{l}}{\text{dim End}(m)}=\underset{n}{\sum}\frac{N^{in}_{l}N^{jk}_{n}}{\text{dim End}(n)}.

(270)

A.4
Quantum dimensions

We define the quantum dimension
d_{i}
of a string
i
as the largest eigenvalue of the fusion matrix
\widehat{\eta}_{i}
, where
\widehat{\eta}_{i}=(\eta^{ij}_{k}:j,k\in\mathcal{S})
and
\eta^{ij}_{k}=
dim
(\Delta^{ij}_{k})
. We define the vector
\left|\omega\right\rangle=\underset{i}{\sum}d_{i}\left|i\right\rangle
as the common eigenvector of
\widehat{\eta}_{i}
, such that

\widehat{\eta}_{i}\left|\omega\right\rangle=d_{i}\left|\omega\right\rangle,

(271)

where
\left|i\right\rangle
is the Dirac notation of string type
i
. Specially, if
i
is a q-type string, we have
\left|i\rangle\langle i|j\right\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\delta_{ij}\left|i\right\rangle
, where
\left|i\rangle\langle i\right|
is a projective operator: when it acts on
\left|i\right\rangle
, it only maps to half of the initial state due to the equivalence relations generated by End
(i)
:

\displaystyle\includegraphics[scale]{F4},

(272)

where we consider the simplest strings in parity-even sector with up to two fermions. For strings with more fermions, we can also divide all the configurations into two sets, and pair one element from each set by the above equivalence relation. This can be concluded as

\left\langle i|i\right\rangle=\frac{1}{\text{dim End}(i)}.

(273)

Remark: The string state
i
here is not the fixed point wavefunction state as above.

The total quantum dimension is defined as the inner product of the common eigenvector:

D^{2}=\left\langle\omega|\omega\right\rangle=\underset{i}{\sum}d_{i}^{2}\left\langle i|i\right\rangle=\underset{i}{\sum}\frac{d_{i}^{2}}{\text{dim End}(i)}.

(274)

From relation in Eq.(
270
), we can derive a general formula for quantum dimensions:

\underset{ml}{\sum}\frac{N^{ij}_{m}N^{mk}_{l}}{\text{dim End}(m)\text{dim End}(l)}\left|\omega\right\rangle=\underset{nl}{\sum}\frac{N^{in}_{l}N^{jk}_{n}}{\text{dim End}(n)\text{dim End}(l)}\left|\omega\right\rangle,

\underset{ml}{\sum}\frac{N^{ij}_{m}\eta^{mk}_{l}}{\text{dim End}(m)}\left|\omega\right\rangle=\underset{nl}{\sum}\eta^{in}_{l}\eta^{jk}_{n}\left|\omega\right\rangle,

\underset{m}{\sum}\frac{N^{ij}_{m}\widehat{\eta}_{m}}{\text{dim End}(m)}\left|\omega\right\rangle=\widehat{\eta}_{i}\widehat{\eta}_{j}\left|\omega\right\rangle,

d_{i}d_{j}=\underset{m}{\sum}\frac{N^{ij}_{m}d_{m}}{\text{dim End}(m)}.

(275)

Appendix B
Fermion Condensation

Anyon condensation is a systematic approach to construct new topological orders from old ones
Bais and Slingerland 2009
;
Eliëns et al. 2014
;
Kong 2014
;
