Burnell 2018
. In the fermionic system, there is an analogous fermion condensation to obtain fermionic topological orders from a bosonic one
Wan and Wang 2017
;
Aasen et al. 2019
;
Lou et al. 2021
.

In this appendix, we will discuss one version of fermion condensation that produces a super fusion category (discussed in Appendix
A
) from a fusion category if there is an object that is promoted to a fermion in the Drinfeld center of the fusion category. We will use this scheme to produce several super fusion categories. We have checked that they all satisfy the conditions summarized in Section
II.11
, although the solutions of which are assumed to be more general than super fusion categories.

B.1
Fermion Condensation Scheme

The easiest way to understand fermion condensation is from the string diagram. We assume that there is a special object
y
in the fusion category
\mathcal{C}
that is promoted to a fermion
(y,\beta_{y})
(
\beta_{y}(x)
is the half braiding of
y
with respect to
x
) in the Drinfeld center
\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{C})
of
\mathcal{C}
. We will denote the fermion string
y
by red color in the string diagram.

In the construction of super fusion category from a fusion category
\mathcal{C}
, we need only the half braiding of
y
with other objects. The string diagram for other objects are still planer. We assume that
y
in
\mathcal{C}
is lifted to a fermion
(y,\beta_{y})
in the Drinfeld center
\mathcal{Z}(\mathcal{C})
. Therefore, a self-twist of
y
will give us a minus fermion sign. The braiding of
y
should satisfy the naturality condition:

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_F_11}}}\ =\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_F_12}}}\ ,\quad\mathrm{for}\ \forall a,b,c.

(276)

It implies that we can move the red
y
string freely under any vertex (morphism) of the diagram.

Since
y^{2}=y\otimes y=1
, the fusion of
y
with the simple objects in
\mathcal{C}
gives us an involution. Since
y
is condensed,
a
and
y\otimes a\in\mathcal{C}
should be identified in the super fusion category
\mathcal{C}/y
. So the simple objects of
\mathcal{C}/y
consist of the orbits of this action. We will denote the representative object of the orbit of
a
as
[a]
, which can be viewed as an object in
\mathcal{C}/y
. If
y\otimes a\cong a\in\mathcal{C}
, then
[a]
is a q-type object in
\mathcal{C}/y
. Otherwise,
[a]
is m-type in
\mathcal{C}/y
.

On the other hand, the hom space of
\mathcal{C}/y
is defined to be

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}/y}([a],[b]):=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}([a],[b])\oplus\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}([a],y\otimes[b]),

(277)

which is a direct sum of bosonic and fermionic fusion spaces. On the right-hand side of the equation,
[a]
is understood as the representative object of the
y
-fusion-action orbit in
\mathcal{C}
. Graphically, the fermionic fusion space (with a black dot on the vertex) of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}/y}([a],[b])
in
\mathcal{C}/y
is defined to be the bosonic fusion space
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}([a],y\otimes[b])
in
\mathcal{C}
as, for example,

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_5}}}:=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_6}}},

(278)

where the red string
y
is fused from left to the vertex in
\mathcal{C}
by convention. The condensed red string of
y
should be understood as under all other strings in the diagram. It is paired up with another red string at the left infinity, such that the total digram is fermion even. The examples we consider in this paper all have trivial Frobenius-Schur indicator for the fermion string:
\varkappa_{y}=1
. For simplicity, we assume that the straight
y
string from leftmost to a vertex can be regularized arbitrarily near the vertex:

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FhalfE6_Fusion_14}}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FhalfE6_Fusion_15}}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FhalfE6_Fusion_16}}}.

(279)

So the notion of horizontal red line makes sense in the string diagram.

In such way, we construct both objects and morphisms of the super fusion category
\mathcal{C}/y
from the fusion category
\mathcal{C}
. This procedure is called fermion condensation. Every string diagram of
\mathcal{C}/y
should be understood as a digram of
\mathcal{C}
with black dots replaced by red strings using the rule Eq. (
278
). In particular, the
F
move of
\mathcal{C}/y
can be derived from the
F
move of the fusion category
\mathcal{C}
.

B.2
F
-move in fermion condensation

For the fermion condensation part, we use a notation of
F
-move differ from Eq.(
23
) in our fixed-point wavefunction approach. In the fermionic theory, the
F
-move is denoted as

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\includegraphics[scale]{F1g}\end{matrix}=\sum_{n\chi\delta}[F^{ijk}_{l}]^{m,\alpha\beta}_{n,\chi\delta}\begin{matrix}\includegraphics[scale]{F2g1}\end{matrix}.

(280)

While in the bosonic theory before fermion condensation, a bosonic
F
-move is denoted in blue color:

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\includegraphics[scale]{F1g}\end{matrix}=\sum_{n\chi\delta}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{ijk}_{l}}]^{m,\alpha\beta}_{n,\chi\delta}\begin{matrix}\includegraphics[scale]{F2g1}\end{matrix}.

(281)

So that if we take the notation
F^{ijk}_{l}
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{ijk}_{l}}
, it is in general a matrix.

B.3
From bosonic to fermionic pentagon equation

In the former section, we have discussed how to obtain a new category
\mathcal{C}/y
from a fusion category
\mathcal{C}
. We have to show that the
\mathcal{C}/y
is indeed a super fusion category. In particular, the new
F
move should satisfy the super (fermionic) pentagon equation, which was first proposed in Ref.
Gu et al. 2015
. Compared to the bosonic counterpart, there is an additional fermion sign in the super pentagon equation.

The super pentagon equation is derived by replacing all the fermionic vertex Hom states (represented by black dots) by red strings of fermions going into left infinity under all other strings. For instance, a diagram with four dots is understood as

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\includegraphics[scale]{4dot}\end{matrix}:=\begin{matrix}\includegraphics[scale]{redline}\end{matrix},

(282)

where the right-hand diagram is a bosonic one for
\mathcal{C}
. The four red fermion strings are paired up from top to bottom.

The string diagrams of a fermionic pentagon equation of
\mathcal{C}/y
can be also expressed as diagrams of
\mathcal{C}
. There are possibly red fermion strings going from a vertex to the left, if the fusion space of the super fusion category is fermionic. If we switch the order (height) of two vertices which are both fermionic, there is a fermion sign

\displaystyle\begin{matrix}\includegraphics[scale]{minus1}\end{matrix}=\begin{matrix}\includegraphics[scale]{minus2}\end{matrix}=(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\begin{matrix}\includegraphics[scale]{minus5}\end{matrix}.

(283)

In general, this fermion sign of the above move is
(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}
. Now the super pentagon equation of the super fusion category is in fact a hexagon equation:

(284)

where the bottom move is the fermion sign move shown in Eq. (
283
). Therefore, we show that the condensed theory
\mathcal{/}y
satisfies the super pentagon equation Eq. (
46
) which is first derived in Ref.
Gu et al. 2015
.

B.4
Equivalence Relations

In doing fermion condensation, we set all transformations related to adding and removing vertices, e.g.
O
-move and
Y
-move, to be normalized as 1,which will cause no harm as the number of vertices is invariant for the initial and final state in any fermion condensation step.

Now we derive the equivalence relations in our fixed-point wavefunction approach (So that we need to put back the notation
\psi_{\text{fix}}
). From our fermion condensation convention, if
m
is q-type, the two equivalent states in Eq.(
9
) and Eq.(
10
) are related by:

\displaystyle\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{f1g4ff}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle:=\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{fcf11}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymk}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\beta}}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{fcf12}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymk}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\beta}}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{F1g}\end{pmatrix},

(285)

\displaystyle\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{f1g2ff}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle:=\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{fcf13}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymk}_{l})_{\beta}}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{fcf14}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymk}_{l})_{\beta}}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{F1g3}\end{pmatrix},

(286)

where
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{y}
denotes for the fermion string in the bosonic theory.

We introduce some of our notations here:

(1) The index in the bottom right corner, e.g.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\beta}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymk}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\beta}}
, refers to that the
F
-move in the uncondensed bosonic theory acts on the vertex with fusion state
\beta
. So that any bosonic
F
-move in such notation is just a phase factor, as the inner states are all fixed as long as the state
\beta
(including the two incoming strings and one outgoing string that define
\beta
) is fixed.

(2) Some of the strings are denoted in red color, e.g. string
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{y{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}m}k}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\beta}}
. A string is denoted in red color if it satisfies the following property:

When it is a trivial string in the fermionic theory, it is a fermion string in the bosonic theory before fermion condensation.

This property is important as it may cause difference on the phase factors in fermion-parity even and odd sectors.

We obtain the relation between the
F
-moves on two equivalent states:

F^{ijm,(\alpha\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{kln,\chi\delta}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymk}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\beta}}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ parity-even}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymk}_{l})_{\beta}}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ parity-odd}\end{array}\right.

(287)

Similarly, if

\displaystyle\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{f2g4ff}\end{pmatrix}

, although the solutions of which are supposed to be more general than super fusion categories.

B.5
Phase factors from fermion condensation

We can obtain explicit forms of the five phase factors in Eq.(
207
)-Eq.(
211
) from fermion condensation:

\displaystyle\Xi^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kl}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymk}_{l})_{\beta}},

(301)

\displaystyle\Xi^{ij}_{kln,\chi\delta}=\Theta_{fi}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yin}_{l}F^{iyn}_{l})_{\delta}},

(302)

\displaystyle\Omega^{kim,\alpha\beta}_{jl}=\Theta_{fi}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yim}_{k}F^{iym}_{k})_{\alpha}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yml}_{j})_{\beta}^{*}},

(303)

\displaystyle\Omega^{ki}_{jln,\chi\delta}=1,

(304)

\displaystyle\Delta^{mji,\alpha\delta}_{nl}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yin}_{l})_{\delta}^{*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yij}_{m})_{\alpha}},

(305)

where the
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{F}
-symbols in blue color are the
F
-symbols in the bosonic theory before fermion condensation. With the above forms of phase factors, we can check that the relations among phase factors in Eq.(
212
)-Eq.(
215
) are satisfied.

We note that the expression for each phase factor can be differ for fermion-parity even and odd sector. Here we do not distinguish parity-even and odd sector for simplicity. But we should keep in mind that the phase factor in parity-even and odd sector can be different, as explicitly shown in Appendix
B.4
.

The five relations among phase factors are obtained from the following relations for
F
-move,
O
-move,
H
-move and a special sequence of moves in Appendix
C
from fermion condensation:

F^{ijm,(\alpha\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{kln,\chi\delta}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymk}_{l})_{\beta}}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type;}

(306)

(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)})^{*}=\Theta_{fi}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yin}_{l}F^{iyn}_{l})_{\delta}}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta})^{*}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type;}

(307)

O^{ij,(\alpha\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{k}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\widetilde{F}^{yij}_{k})_{\alpha}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yij}_{k})_{\beta}}O^{ij,\alpha\beta}_{k}\text{, \ \ if }k\text{ is q-type;}

(308)

Y^{ij}_{k,(\alpha\cdot f)(\beta\cdot f)}=Y^{ij}_{k,\alpha\beta}\text{, \ \ if }k\text{ is q-type;}

(309)

\widetilde{H}^{imk,(\alpha\times f)\delta}_{lnj,(\beta\times f)\chi}=\Theta_{fi}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yim}_{k}F^{iym}_{k})_{\alpha}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\widetilde{F}^{yml}_{j})_{\beta}}\widetilde{H}^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type;}

(310)

(\widetilde{H}^{imk,\alpha(\delta\times f)}_{lnj,\beta(\chi\times f)})^{*}=(\widetilde{H}^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi})^{*}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type;}

(311)

F^{ijm,(\alpha\times f)\beta}_{kln,\chi(\delta\times f)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yin}_{l})_{\delta}^{*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yij}_{m})_{\alpha}}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ if }i\text{ is q-type.}

(312)

where we derive these relations in Appendix
B.4
and Appendix
C
.

We take the gauge in Eq.(
77
) on the relation in Eq.(
308
),

O^{ij,(\alpha\times f)}_{k}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\widetilde{F}^{yij}_{k})_{\alpha,\underline{\alpha}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yij}_{k})_{\alpha,\underline{\beta}}}O^{ij,\alpha}_{k}\text{, \ \ if }k\text{ is q-type,}

(313)

where we note that the bosonic
F
-move
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\widetilde{F}^{yij}_{k})_{\alpha,\underline{\alpha}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yij}_{k})_{\alpha,\underline{\beta}}}
acts on different vertices, but the two vertices are in the same space spanned by
\alpha
and
\alpha\times f
. In order not to cause confusion, we in addition label the vertices
\underline{\alpha}
and
\underline{\beta}
in Eq. (
313
).
We take the gauge in Eq.(
79
) on Eq. (
309
),

Y^{ij}_{k,(\alpha\cdot f)}=Y^{ij}_{k,\alpha}\text{, \ \ if }k\text{ is q-type,}

(314)

Combining Eq.(
310
), Eq.(
311
), Eq.(
313
), Eq.(
314
) and a relation among
F
-move,
O
-move and
H
-move in Eq.(
134
), we obtain two more relations between
F
-moves:

F^{imk,(\alpha\times f)\delta}_{lnj,(\beta\times f)\chi}=\Theta_{fi}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yim}_{k}F^{iym}_{k})_{\alpha}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yml}_{j})_{\beta}^{*}}F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi}\text{, \ \ if }m\text{ is q-type;}

(315)

(F^{imk,\alpha(\delta\times f)}_{lnj,\beta(\chi\times f)})^{*}=(F^{imk,\alpha\delta}_{lnj,\beta\chi})^{*}\text{, \ \ if }n\text{ is q-type.}

(316)

Therefore, relations in Eq.(
306
), Eq.(
307
), Eq.(
315
),Eq.(
316
) and Eq.(
312
) exactly give the forms of the five phase factors in Eq.(
301
)-Eq.(
305
).

B.6
Fermionic topological order
SO(3)_{6}/\psi
from
SO(3)_{6}

B.6.1
SO(3)_{6}
string-net model

The full data for the bosonic
SO(3)_{6}
theory are listed in, for example, Ref .
Fidkowski et al. 2013
. There are 4 string types or simple objects
1,s,\tilde{s},\psi
in
SO(3)_{6}
. Some important fusion rules are
s\times\psi=\tilde{s},\psi\times\psi=1,s\times s=1+s+\tilde{s}
, from which we can derive all other fusion rules. The quantum dimensions of the simple objects are
d_{1}=d_{\psi}=1
,
d_{s}=d_{\tilde{s}}=1+\sqrt{2}
. The object
\psi
is a fermion (in together with half-braiding) in modular tensor category
\mathcal{Z}(SO(3)_{6})
. So we can try to condense the fermion
\psi
to obtain a super fusion category
SO(3)_{6}/\psi
.

B.6.2
Fermionic topological order
SO(3)_{6}/\psi

Since the nontrivial simple objects
s
and
\tilde{s}
are changed into each other under the fusion of
y
:
s\times\psi=\tilde{s}
, they become the same simple object (which is also denoted as
s
) after the fermion condensation. So the simple objects of
SO(3)_{6}/\psi
are
1
and
s
. The quantum dimensions of them are
d_{1}=1
and
d_{s}=1+\sqrt{2}
.

If we use dashed line and solid line to indicate the simple objects
1
and
s
, the fusion rules of
SO(3)_{6}/\psi
are

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_SO36_fusion_1}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_SO36_fusion_2}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_SO36_fusion_3}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_SO36_fusion_4}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_SO36_fusion_5}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_SO36_fusion_6}}}\ ,\ \

(317)

where the last three fusion diagram of
SO(3)_{6}/\psi
come from the fusion rule
s\times s=1+s+\psi\times s
in
SO(3)_{6}
. Only the last one is fermionic with a black dot which should be understood as a red string of
\psi
going out of the vertex.

B.7
Majorana toric code from Ising string-net model

B.7.1
Ising string-net model

Ising string-net model is one of the simplest models with non-Abelian fusion rules. The Ising fusion category has three simple objects
\{1,\psi,\sigma\}
with quantum dimensions
d_{1}=d_{\psi}=1
,
d_{\sigma}=\sqrt{2}
. The fusion rules of them are:
\psi\times\psi=1
,
\psi\times\sigma=\sigma\times\psi=\sigma
,
\sigma\times\sigma=1+\psi
. If we use dashed, red and blue lines to represent
1
,
\psi
and
\sigma
, the trivalent vertices of fusion rules are

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Ising_fusion_1}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Ising_fusion_2}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Ising_fusion_3}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Ising_fusion_4}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Ising_fusion_5}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Ising_fusion_6}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Ising_fusion_7}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Ising_fusion_8}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Ising_fusion_9}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Ising_fusion_10}}}.

(330)

The
F
symbols of the Ising fusion category can be found in, for example, Ref.
Kitaev 2006
.

B.7.2
Majorana toric code model

Now we want to condense the fermion
\psi
(in together with half-braiding) in the Drinfeld center of Ising fusion category to obtain a super fusion category. After the condensation, we have two simple objects 0 and 1, which come from 1 and
\sigma
in the Ising model. The quantum dimension of them are
d_{0}=1
,
d_{1}=\sqrt{2}
. The fusion rules of them are

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_1}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_2}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_3}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_4}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_5}}}:=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_6}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_7}}}:=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_8}}}\ ,\ \ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_9}}}:=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_Maj_Fusion_10}}},

(331)

with fusion coefficient
N_{00}^{0}=1
,
N_{01}^{1}=N_{10}^{1}=N_{11}^{0}=2
. The black dot in the diagram means that the fermion is condensed at this vertex, which can be understood as a fermion string going out of the vertex from the bosonic string diagram. Since we have fusion rule
\psi\times\sigma=\sigma
in the Ising model, the nontrivial object 1 in the super fusion category is a q-type one. The fusion rule
\sigma\times\sigma=1+\psi
in the Ising model becomes the fusion of
1
and
1
into
0
with one bosonic channel and one fermionic channel in the super fusion category.

The
F

symbol is of full rank. The full rank unitary

B.8
Fermionic topological order
\left(\frac{1}{2}E_{6}\right)/y
from
\frac{1}{2}E_{6}

B.8.1
Unitary fusion category
\frac{1}{2}E_{6}

The unitary fusion category
\frac{1}{2}E_{6}
does not admit a braiding structure. It has three simple objects:
1
,
x
,
y
. The fusion rules are given by:
x\times x=1+2x+y
,
x\times y=y\times x=x
and
y\times y=1
. If we represent
1
,
x
and
y
by dotted, blue and red strings respectively, the fusion configurations can be shown as:

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_Fusion_1}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_Fusion_2}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_Fusion_3}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_Fusion_4}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_Fusion_5}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_Fusion_6}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_Fusion_7}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_Fusion_8}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_Fusion_9}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_Fusion_10}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_Fusion_11}}}.

(340)

The last fusion of
x
and
x
to
y
has two fusion channels. So we use a vertex index
\alpha=1,2
. All the simple objects are self-dual. The quantum dimensions can be calculated from the fusion rules as:
d_{1}=d_{y}=1
,
d_{x}=1+\sqrt{3}
.

The
F
matrices in this tensor category are summarized as (
\sigma^{i}
’s are Pauli matrices)
Hong et al. 2008

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{abc}_{d}}

\displaystyle=1,\quad\text{ if $a=1$ or $b=1$ or $c=1$},

(341)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{yyy}_{y}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{xyy}_{x}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{yyx}_{x}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{xyx}_{1}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{yxx}_{1}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{yxx}_{y}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{xxy}_{1}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{xxy}_{y}}=1,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F^{xxx}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\sqrt{3}-1}{2}&\frac{\sqrt{3}-1}{2}&\frac{\sqrt{\sqrt{3}-1}}{2}e^{\pi i/6}&\frac{\sqrt{\sqrt{3}-1}}{2}e^{2\pi i/3}&\frac{\sqrt{\sqrt{3}-1}}{2}e^{2\pi i/3}&\frac{\sqrt{\sqrt{3}-1}}{2}e^{\pi i/6}\\

\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_F_5}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_F_6}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_F_7}}}\right)^{T}
and
\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_F_8}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_F_9}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_F_10}}}\right)^{T}
respectively. Note that the last diagram with
\alpha
and
\beta
is in fact a vector of four bases with the usual tensor product order, i.e.,
(\alpha,\beta)=(1,1),(1,2),(2,1),(2,2)
. One can check that the above
F
moves satisfy (bosonic) pentagon equations.

To condense the fermion
y
, we first have to show that
y
is indeed lifted to a fermion in the Drinfeld center of the fusion category
\frac{1}{2}E_{6}
. In fact, by solving the naturality condition in Eq. (
276
), we have the following half-braiding of
y
:

\displaystyle\beta_{y}(1)=1,\quad\beta_{y}(y)=-v^{yy}_{1}\circ v^{1}_{yy},\quad\beta_{y}(x)=iv^{xy}_{x}\circ v^{x}_{yx},

(347)

where
v^{c}_{ab}
is the basis of morphism in
\textrm{Hom}(a\times b,c)
, and
v_{c}^{ba}
is the dual. Graphically, the half-braiding induces the relations: (we use the convention that the
y
string in
\beta_{y}(a)
is the under-crossing line)

\displaystyle\beta_{y}(y)

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_ey_1}}}=-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_ey_2}}},

(348)

\displaystyle\beta_{y}(x)

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_ey_3}}}=i\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_halfE6_ey_4}}}.

(349)

The first half-braiding equation implies that
(y,\beta_{y})
is indeed a fermion in
\mathcal{Z}(\frac{1}{2}E_{6})
. We can condense this fermion to obtain a super fusion category.

B.8.2
Fermionic topological order
\left(\frac{1}{2}E_{6}\right)/\psi

After the fermion condensation, the simple objects are 0 and 1 from the objects
1
and

in the fusion category
\frac{1}{2}E_{6}
. The quantum dimensions are

\end{pmatrix}

to be one of the

\displaystyle\quad\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FhalfE6_F_73}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FhalfE6_F_74}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FhalfE6_F_75}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FhalfE6_F_76}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FhalfE6_F_77}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FhalfE6_F_78}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FhalfE6_F_79}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FhalfE6_F_80}}}\end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=

\end{pmatrix}

(365)

One can show that
F^{111}_{1}
has rank 12.

We have checked that the above
F
matrices satisfy the consistent equations such as the super pentagon equation.

B.9
Fermionic topological order from Tambara-Yamagami category for
\mathbb{Z}_{2N}

B.9.1
Unitary fusion category
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}

The fusion category
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}
is the Tambara-Yamagami category
Tambara and Yamagami 1998
for
\mathbb{Z}_{2N}
with symmetric non-degenerate bicharacter of type
t
(
1\leq t\leq 2N-1
and
\operatorname{gcd}(t,2N)=1
) defined as

\displaystyle\chi_{t}(a,b)=e^{2\pi itab/(2N)}.

(366)

The simple objects are labelled by
\mathbb{Z}_{2N}\cup\{\sigma\}
where
\mathbb{Z}_{2N}=\{0,1,\cdots,2N-1\}
is the cyclic group of order
2N
.
\sigma
is an additional object. The quantum dimensions of them are
d_{i}=1
(
\forall i\in\mathbb{Z}_{2N}
) and
d_{\sigma}=\sqrt{2N}
. The fusion rule for objects in
\mathbb{Z}_{2N}
is simply the addition modulo
2N
:
a\times b=[a+b]_{2N}
. The
\sigma
object can absorb all
\mathbb{Z}_{2N}
objects:
a\times\sigma=\sigma\times a=\sigma
. The fusion of
\sigma
with itself is
\sigma\times\sigma=\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}a
. If we use red strings and blue string to denote the simple objects in
\mathbb{Z}_{2N}
and
\sigma
, then the fusion rules can be represented as

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_TY_Fusion_1}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_TY_Fusion_2}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_TY_Fusion_3}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_TY_Fusion_4}}}\ .

(367)

The nontrivial
F
matrices for the fusion category
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}
are related to the bicharacter
\chi_{t}
defined in Eq. (
366
) as:

\displaystyle=F^{a\sigma b}_{\sigma}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_TY_F_2}}}\ ,\ \quad F^{a\sigma b}_{\sigma}=\chi_{t}(a,b),

(368)

\displaystyle=F^{\sigma a\sigma}_{b}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_TY_F_4}}}\ ,\ \quad F^{\sigma a\sigma}_{b}=\chi_{t}(a,b),

(369)

\displaystyle=\sum_{b}(F^{\sigma\sigma\sigma}_{\sigma})_{ab}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_TY_F_6}}}\ ,\ \quad(F^{\sigma\sigma\sigma}_{\sigma})_{ab}=\frac{\varkappa}{\sqrt{2N}}\chi_{t}(a,b)^{-1},

(370)

where
\varkappa=\pm 1
is the Frobenius-Schur indicator of simple object
\sigma
.

B.9.2
Fermionic topological order
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}/\psi_{N}
(
N
odd)

To perform fermion condensation in the category
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}
, we have to find a fermion in the Drinfeld center of the category. Let us try to find the half-braiding of the object
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{2N}
. Direct calculations of the naturality condition Eq. (
276
) for the half-braiding of
N
give the results:

\displaystyle\beta_{N}(a)

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_TY_halfbraiding_1}}}=(-1)^{a}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_TY_halfbraiding_2}}}\ ,

(371)

\displaystyle\beta_{N}(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_TY_halfbraiding_3}}}=i\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_TY_halfbraiding_4}}}\ .

(372)

Now let us assume that
N
is an odd integer. Then the object
(N,\beta_{N})
in
\mathcal{Z}\left(\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}\right)
has twist
\theta_{(N,\beta_{N})}=\beta_{N}(N)=-1
and is a fermion. Therefore, we can condense the fermion
\psi_{N}=N
in
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}
.

After the fermion condensation, the object
a
and
[a+N]_{2N}
in
\mathbb{Z}_{2N}
of
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}
are identified. So the simple objects in the super fusion category
\mathrm{TY}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2N}}^{t,\varkappa}/\psi_{N}
are
0,1,\cdots,N-1,\sigma
. The fusion rules of them can be represented as

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_Fusion1_1}}}\text{if }a+b<N,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_Fusion1_2}}}\text{if }a+b\geq N,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_Fusion1_3}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_Fusion1_4}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_Fusion1_5}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_Fusion1_6}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_Fusion1_7}}}\ ,\ \vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_Fusion1_8}}}\ ,\

(373)

where
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}
are objects from
\mathbb{Z}_{2N}
, and
\sigma
is the same one in the fusion category. Every vertex of the above fusion rules can be either bosonic or fermionic.

From the
F
matrices of the original fusion category, we can obtain the
F
matrices of the super fusion category as

\displaystyle=F^{000}_{0}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_2}}}\ ,\ \quad F^{000}_{0}=1,

(374)

\displaystyle=F^{abc}_{[a+b+c]_{N}}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_4}}}\ ,\ \quad F^{abc}_{[a+b+c]_{N}}=(-1)^{a\left\lfloor\frac{b+c}{N}\right\rfloor}.

(375)

If
a+b<N
, the
F^{ab\sigma}_{\sigma}
-type and
F^{\sigma ab}_{\sigma}
-type
F
moves are

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_5}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_6}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=F^{ab\sigma}_{\sigma}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_7}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_8}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_9}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_10}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T},

\displaystyle F^{ab\sigma}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&(-1)^{a}\\
0&1&(-1)^{a}&0\\
\end{pmatrix},

(376)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_11}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_12}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_13}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_14}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=F^{\sigma ab}_{\sigma}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_15}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_16}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T},

\displaystyle F^{\sigma ab}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\\
0&(-1)^{b}\\
(-1)^{b}&0\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(377)

On the other hand, if
a+b\geq N
, the
F
moves are

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_17}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_18}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=F^{ab\sigma}_{\sigma}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_19}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_20}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_21}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_22}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T},

\displaystyle F^{ab\sigma}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}0&1&(-1)^{a}&0\\
1&0&0&(-1)^{a}\\
\end{pmatrix},

(378)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_23}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_24}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_25}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_26}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=F^{\sigma ab}_{\sigma}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_27}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_28}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T},

\displaystyle F^{\sigma ab}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}0&(-1)^{a+b}i\\
(-1)^{a+b}i&1\\
(-1)^{a}i&0\\
0&(-1)^{a}i\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(379)

They are all projective
F
matrices with rank 2.
The
F^{a\sigma b}_{\sigma}
-type
F
move is

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\!\!\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_29}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_30}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_31}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_32}}}\ \!\!\end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=F^{a\sigma b}_{\sigma}\begin{pmatrix}\!\!\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_33}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_34}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_35}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_36}}}\ \!\!\end{pmatrix}^{T},

\displaystyle F^{a\sigma b}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi_{t}(a,b)}{2}\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&(-1)^{a}\\
0&1&(-1)^{a}&0\\
0&(-1)^{b}&(-1)^{a+b}&0\\
(-1)^{b}&0&0&(-1)^{a+b}\end{pmatrix},

(380)

which also has rank 2. For the
F^{\sigma\sigma a}_{b}
-type and
F^{a\sigma\sigma}_{b}
-type
F
moves, we need to compare
a
and
b
. If
b\geq a
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_37}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_38}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=F^{\sigma\sigma a}_{b}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_39}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_40}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_41}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_42}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T},

\displaystyle F^{\sigma\sigma a}_{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&(-1)^{b+1}i\\
0&1&(-1)^{b}i&0\\
\end{pmatrix},

(381)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_43}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_44}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_45}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_46}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=F^{a\sigma\sigma}_{b}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_47}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_48}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T},

\displaystyle F^{a\sigma\sigma}_{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&(-1)^{a}\\
0&(-1)^{a}\\
1&0\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(382)

If
b<a
, on the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_49}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_50}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=F^{\sigma\sigma a}_{b}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_51}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_52}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_53}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_54}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T},

\displaystyle F^{\sigma\sigma a}_{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}0&1&(-1)^{b}i&0\\
1&0&0&(-1)^{b+1}i\\
\end{pmatrix},

(383)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_55}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_56}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_57}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_58}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=F^{a\sigma\sigma}_{b}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_59}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_60}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T},

\displaystyle F^{a\sigma\sigma}_{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}0&(-1)^{a}\\
1&0\\
1&0\\
0&(-1)^{a}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(384)

Finally, the
F
moves of types
F^{\sigma a\sigma}_{b}
and
F^{\sigma\sigma\sigma}_{\sigma}
are

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_61}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_62}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_63}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_64}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=F^{\sigma a\sigma}_{b}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_65}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_66}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_67}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_68}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T},

\displaystyle F^{\sigma a\sigma}_{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi_{t}(a,b)}{2}\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&(-1)^{b+1}i\\
0&(-1)^{a}&(-1)^{a+b}i&0\\
0&(-1)^{a}&(-1)^{a+b}i&0\\
1&0&0&(-1)^{b+1}i\end{pmatrix},

(385)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_69}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_70}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_71}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_72}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}}(F^{\sigma\sigma\sigma}_{\sigma})_{ab}\begin{pmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_73}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_74}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_75}}}\ ,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{Fig_FTY_FF_76}}}\ \end{pmatrix}^{T},

\displaystyle(F^{\sigma\sigma\sigma}_{\sigma})_{ab}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varkappa}{\sqrt{2N}}\chi_{t}^{-1}(a,b)\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&(-1)^{a+b+1}i\\
0&1&(-1)^{a+b+1}i&0\\
0&(-1)^{b}&(-1)^{a}i&0\\
(-1)^{b}&0&0&(-1)^{a}i\end{pmatrix}.

(386)

We have checked that the above
F
matrices satisfy fermionic pentagon equations.

Appendix C
Equivalence relation for diagonal fusion states

To obtain relation in Eq.(
213
), we need to consider the following sequence of
F
-move and
O
-moves:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{A1}\end{pmatrix}\simeq\frac{n_{n}}{n_{k}}\mathcal{F}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\mathcal{O}^{jk,\chi}_{n}\mathcal{O}^{in,\delta}_{l}(\mathcal{O}^{mk,\beta}_{l})^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{jn,\chi}_{k})^{-1}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{A2}\end{pmatrix},

(387)

where the Majorana numbers on the gauged
O
-move is still assigned from top to bottom, e.g.,
\mathcal{O}^{jn,\chi}_{k}=\theta^{s(\chi)}_{\underline{\chi}}\theta^{s(\chi)}_{\underline{\chi^{\prime}}}O^{jn,\chi}_{k}
. The above equation is derived by

\displaystyle\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{A1}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\simeq\sum_{n^{\prime}\chi^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}}\mathcal{F}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{A3}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathcal{F}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{A3nof}\end{pmatrix}+(n_{n}-1)\mathcal{F}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{A3ff}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathcal{F}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\mathcal{O}^{jk,\chi}_{n}\mathcal{O}^{in,\delta}_{l}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{iline}\end{pmatrix}+(n_{n}-1)\mathcal{F}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)}\widetilde{O_{1}}^{jk,\chi(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)}_{nil,\delta}\mathcal{O}^{in,\delta}_{l}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{iline}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\simeq n_{n}\mathcal{F}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\mathcal{O}^{jk,\chi}_{n}\mathcal{O}^{in,\delta}_{l}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{iline}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{n_{n}}{n_{k}}\mathcal{F}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\mathcal{O}^{jk,\chi}_{n}\mathcal{O}^{in,\delta}_{l}(\mathcal{O}^{mk,\beta}_{l})^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{jn,\chi}_{k})^{-1}\Psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{A2}\end{pmatrix},

(388)

where in the second line the factor
(n_{n}-1)
means that the term
\mathcal{F}^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,(\chi\times f)(\delta\times f)}
only exist when
n_{n}=2
. In the final line we have in addition a factor
\frac{1}{n_{k}}
, as when
k
is q-type, the inverse
O
-move
(\mathcal{O}^{jn,\chi}_{k})^{-1}
can only map to one-half of the state (only one of the two equivalent states).

From Eq.(
387
), we can obtain the relation in Eq.(
211
) that is needed to derive all 2-3 moves in the fermionic partition function:

F^{ijm,(\alpha\times f)\beta}_{kln,\chi(\delta\times f)}=\Delta^{mji,\alpha\delta}_{nl}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ if }i\text{ is q-type},

(389)

where the Majorana numbers are removed when considering equivalence relations.

From fermion condensation, if
i
is q-type, we have the following equivalent relations for fermion parity-even and odd sector respectively:

\displaystyle\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{ac1}\end{pmatrix}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\widetilde{F}^{yin}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\delta}}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{ac2}\end{pmatrix}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\widetilde{F}^{yin}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\delta}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{y{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}i}j}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}m}})_{\alpha}}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{ac3}\end{pmatrix},

(390)

\displaystyle\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{ac4}\end{pmatrix}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\widetilde{F}^{yin}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\delta}}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{ac5}\end{pmatrix}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\widetilde{F}^{yin}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\delta}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{y{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}i}j}_{m})_{\alpha}}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{ac6}\end{pmatrix}.

(391)

When the fermion parity on
\alpha
and
\delta
are changed, the following
O
-move will also induce a phase factor:

O^{in,(\delta\times f)}_{l}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\widetilde{F}^{yin}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\delta}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{y{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}i}n}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\delta}}O^{in,\delta}_{l}\text{, \ \ parity-even}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(\widetilde{F}^{yin}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\delta}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{y{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}i}n}_{l})_{\delta}}O^{in,\delta}_{l}\text{, \ \ parity-odd}\end{array}\right.

(392)

Therefore, we obtain another equivalence relation for
F
-move if string
i
is q-type:

F^{ijm,(\alpha\times f)\beta}_{kln,\chi(\delta\times f)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{y{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}i}n}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}l}})_{\delta}^{*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{y{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}i}j}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}m}})_{\alpha}}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ parity-even}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{y{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}i}n}_{l})_{\delta}^{*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{y{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}i}j}_{m})_{\alpha}}F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{kln,\chi\delta}\text{, \ \ parity-odd}\end{array}\right.

(393)

which corresponds to the fermion parity change on two diagonal fusion states
\alpha
and
\delta
.

Appendix D
Check the consistency between the 2-3 moves and the projective unitary conditions

D.1
Obtain the second 2-3 move

To obtain the second 2-3 move Eq.(
196
), we consider two cases:

(1) Let the string
t^{\prime}
be m-type. We multiply by
(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}
and sum over
m,\alpha,\beta
on both sides of the standard 2-3 move Eq.(
195
):

\displaystyle\underset{\epsilon,m\alpha\beta}{\sum}(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}=\underset{t\eta\psi\kappa,m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{t}}{n_{t}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi},

(394)

where

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{t}d_{m}}{n_{t}n_{m}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}=\delta_{tt^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{\psi\psi^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }t^{\prime}\text{ is m-type.}

(395)

We can get Eq.(
196
) straightforwardly.

(2) Let the string
t^{\prime}
be q-type. We multiply by
\frac{1}{2}[(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}+(\Xi^{ij}_{knt^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt^{\prime},(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\psi^{\prime}\times f)})^{*}]
and sum over
m,\alpha,\beta
on both sides of Eq.(
195
):

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \underset{\epsilon,m\alpha\beta}{\sum}(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{1}{2}[(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}+(\Xi^{ij}_{knt^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt^{\prime},(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\psi^{\prime}\times f)})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}]

\displaystyle=\underset{t\eta\psi\kappa,m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{t}}{n_{t}}\frac{1}{2}[(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}+(\Xi^{ij}_{knt^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt^{\prime},(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\psi^{\prime}\times f)})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}],

(396)

where the second projective unitary condition for
G
-move in Eq.(
204
) is applied:

\underset{m\alpha\beta}{\sum}\frac{d_{t}d_{m}}{n_{t}n_{m}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi}=\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{tt^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{\psi\psi^{\prime}}+(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}\delta_{tt^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta(\eta^{\prime}\times f)}\delta_{\psi(\psi^{\prime}\times f)})\text{, \ \ if }t^{\prime}\text{ is q-type.}

(397)

where we note that the phase factor here
(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}=(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,(\eta\times f)(\psi\times f)})^{*}=\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta\psi}
. Then

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \underset{m\alpha\beta\epsilon}{\sum}(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{d_{m}}{n_{m}}\frac{1}{2}[(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}+(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\psi^{\prime}\times f)})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}]

\displaystyle\ \ =\underset{\kappa}{\sum}\frac{1}{2}[G^{itn,\psi^{\prime}\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta^{\prime}\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}+(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}G^{itn,(\psi^{\prime}\times f)\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt^{\prime},(\eta^{\prime}\times f)\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}],

(398)

where the two terms on the left hand side are actually equal, as well as the two terms on the right hand side:

\displaystyle(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}

\displaystyle=(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\psi^{\prime}\times f)})^{*},

\displaystyle G^{itn,\psi^{\prime}\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta^{\prime}\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}

\displaystyle=(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}G^{itn,(\psi^{\prime}\times f)\chi}_{lps,(\kappa\times f)\gamma}G^{jkt,(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\kappa\times f)}_{lsq,\delta\phi},

(399)

where as we require each
G
-move preserve fermion parity, the term
G^{itn,(\psi^{\prime}\times f)\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,(\eta^{\prime}\times f)\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}
actually varies the fermion parity on
\kappa
and should be written as
G^{itn,(\psi^{\prime}\times f)\chi}_{lps,(\kappa\times f)\gamma}G^{jkt,(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\kappa\times f)}_{lsq,\delta\phi}
. The first equality in Eq.(
399
) is exactly the complex conjugate of the second equivalence relation on
G
-move Eq.(
208
). The second equality in Eq.(
399
) is satisfies straightforwardly by the relation among phase factors in Eq.(
212
):

\displaystyle(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}=(\Omega^{nit,\psi^{\prime}\kappa}_{sl})^{*}(\Xi^{jkt,\eta^{\prime}\kappa}_{ls})^{*},

(400)

where
G^{itn,\psi^{\prime}\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}=(\Omega^{nit,\psi^{\prime}\kappa}_{sl})^{*}G^{itn,(\psi^{\prime}\times f)\chi}_{lps,(\kappa\times f)\gamma}
, and
G^{jkt,\eta^{\prime}\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}=(\Xi^{jkt,\eta^{\prime}\kappa}_{ls})^{*}G^{jkt,(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\kappa\times f)}_{lsq,\delta\phi}
. We can again check this relation from fermion condensation:
\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}}=\Theta_{fi}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yit}_{n}F^{iyt}_{n})_{\psi^{\prime}}}
,
\Omega^{nit,\psi^{\prime}\kappa}_{sl}=\Theta_{fi}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{yit}_{n}F^{iyt}_{n})_{\psi^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ytl}_{s})_{\kappa}^{*}}

and
\Xi^{jkt,\eta^{\prime}\kappa}_{ls}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ytl}_{s})_{\kappa}}
, we find Eq.(
400
) is satisfied straightforwardly.

Physically, from the point of view of fixed-point wavefunctions, there is a splitting on the two channels. Suppose that
\eta
and
\psi
are two bosonic fusion states. In terms of
F
-moves, the splitting of Eq.(
398
) is graphically represented as:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{pent4g}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\simeq\sum_{\kappa}\frac{1}{2}F^{itn,\psi^{\prime}\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}F^{jkt,\eta^{\prime}\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{pent3g}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\simeq\underset{m\alpha\beta\epsilon}{\sum}(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{1}{2}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}F^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}F^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{pent3g}\end{pmatrix},

(401)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{pent4gf}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\kappa}\frac{1}{2}(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}F^{itn,(\psi^{\prime}\times f)\chi}_{lps,(\kappa\times f)\gamma}F^{jkt,(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\kappa\times f)}_{lsq,\delta\phi}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{pent3g}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\underset{m\alpha\beta\epsilon}{\sum}(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{1}{2}(\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}})^{*}(F^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\psi^{\prime}\times f)})^{*}F^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}F^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}\psi_{\text{fix}}\begin{pmatrix}\includegraphics[scale]{pent3g}\end{pmatrix}.

(402)

Therefore, Eq.(
398
) again implies:

\underset{m\alpha\beta\epsilon}{\sum}(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\frac{d_{m}}{n_{m}}(G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon}=\underset{\kappa}{\sum}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}.

(403)

D.2
Obtain the third 2-3 move

To obtain the third 2-3 move Eq.(
197
), we consider two cases:

(1) Let the string
m^{\prime}
be m-type. We multiply by
(G^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}
and sum over
s,\phi,\gamma
on both sides of the second 2-3 move Eq.(
196
). We can obtain Eq.(
197
) by applying

\underset{s\phi\gamma}{\sum}\frac{d_{s}d_{m}}{n_{s}n_{m}}(G^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}=\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}}\text{, \ \ if }m^{\prime}\text{ is m-type.}

(404)

(2) Let the string
m^{\prime}
be q-type. We multiply by
\frac{1}{2}[(G^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}+\Xi^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qp}(G^{ijm^{\prime},(\alpha^{\prime}\times f)(\epsilon^{\prime}\times f)}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}]
and sum over
s,\phi,\gamma
on both sides of Eq.(
196
):

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \underset{s\phi\kappa\gamma}{\sum}\frac{d_{s}}{n_{s}}\frac{1}{2}(G^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi}+\Xi^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qp}(G^{ijm^{\prime},(\alpha^{\prime}\times f)(\epsilon^{\prime}\times f)}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa}_{lsq,\delta\phi})

\displaystyle\ \ =(-1)^{s(\alpha)s(\delta)}\underset{\beta}{\sum}\frac{1}{2}((G^{ijm,\alpha^{\prime}\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon^{\prime}}+\Xi^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qp}(G^{ijm,(\alpha^{\prime}\times f)\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta(\epsilon^{\prime}\times f)}).

(405)

Splitting the two channels, we again get Eq.(
197
) by applying

\underset{s\phi\gamma}{\sum}\frac{d_{s}d_{m}}{n_{s}n_{m}}G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma}(G^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}=\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}}+\Xi^{ijm^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qp}\delta_{mm^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha(\alpha^{\prime}\times f)}\delta_{\epsilon(\epsilon^{\prime}\times f)})\text{, \ \ if }m^{\prime}\text{ is q-type.}

(406)

We obtain Eq.(
197
) from Eq.(
196
) by requiring Eq.(
203
) and the relation in Eq.(
213
):

\Xi^{ijm,\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qp}=\Xi^{ijm,\alpha^{\prime}\beta}_{kn}(\Delta^{nkm,\beta\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qp})^{*},

(407)

as from which we have

\displaystyle(G^{ijm,\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*}

\displaystyle=\Xi^{ijm,\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qp}(G^{ijm,(\alpha^{\prime}\times f)(\epsilon^{\prime}\times f)}_{qps,\phi\gamma})^{*},

\displaystyle(G^{ijm,\alpha^{\prime}\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\Xi^{ijm,\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qp}(G^{ijm,(\alpha^{\prime}\times f)(\beta\times f)}_{knt,\eta\psi})^{*}G^{mkn,(\beta\times f)\chi}_{lpq,\delta(\epsilon^{\prime}\times f)}.

(408)

We can again check Eq.(
407
) from fermion condensation:
\Xi^{ijm,\alpha^{\prime}\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qp}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymq}_{p})_{\epsilon^{\prime}}}
,
\Xi^{ijm,\alpha^{\prime}\beta}_{kn}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymk}_{n})_{\beta}}
and
\Delta^{nkm,\beta\epsilon^{\prime}}_{qp}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymq}_{p})_{\epsilon^{\prime}}^{*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(F^{ymk}_{n})_{\beta}}
.

D.3
Obtain the rest five 2-3 moves

Similarly, we obtain Eq.(
198
) from Eq.(
197
) by requiring Eq.(
204
) and
(\Xi^{it}_{lps,\kappa^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}})^{*}=(\Xi^{ij}_{qps,\phi\gamma^{\prime}})^{*}\Omega^{tj}_{qls,\kappa^{\prime}\phi}
, where

\displaystyle(G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}})^{*}

\displaystyle=(\Xi^{it}_{lps,\kappa^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}})^{*}(G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,(\kappa^{\prime}\times f)(\gamma^{\prime}\times f)})^{*},

\displaystyle(G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi\gamma^{\prime}})^{*}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa^{\prime}}_{lsq,\delta\phi}

\displaystyle=(\Xi^{ij}_{qps,\phi\gamma^{\prime}})^{*}\Omega^{tj}_{qls,\kappa^{\prime}\phi}(G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,(\phi\times f)(\gamma^{\prime}\times f)})^{*}G^{jkt,\eta(\kappa^{\prime}\times f)}_{lsq,\delta(\phi\times f)}.

(409)

We obtain Eq.(
199
) from Eq.(
195
) by requiring Eq.(
203
) and
\Xi^{jkt,\eta^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}}_{ls}=\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi}(\Omega^{nit,\psi\kappa^{\prime}}_{sl})^{*}
(the same relation as Eq.(
400
)), where

\displaystyle(G^{jkt,\eta^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}}_{lsq,\delta\phi})^{*}

\displaystyle=\Xi^{jkt,\eta^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}}_{ls}(G^{jkt,(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\kappa\times f)}_{lsq,\delta\phi})^{*},

\displaystyle G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa^{\prime}\gamma}

\displaystyle=\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta^{\prime}\psi}(\Omega^{nit,\psi\kappa^{\prime}}_{sl})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,(\eta^{\prime}\times f)(\psi\times f)}G^{itn,(\psi\times f)\chi}_{lps,(\kappa^{\prime}\times f)\gamma}.

(410)

We obtain Eq.(
200
) from Eq.(
195
) by requiring Eq.(
205
) and
\Omega^{nit,\psi^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}}_{sl}=\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta\psi^{\prime}}(\Xi^{jkt,\eta\kappa^{\prime}}_{ls})^{*}
(the same relation as Eq.(
400
)), where

\displaystyle(G^{itn,\psi^{\prime}\chi}_{lps,\kappa^{\prime}\gamma})^{*}

\displaystyle=\Omega^{nit,\psi^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}}_{sl}(G^{itn,(\psi^{\prime}\times f)\chi}_{lps,(\kappa^{\prime}\times f)\gamma})^{*},

\displaystyle G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,\eta\psi^{\prime}}G^{jkt,\eta\kappa^{\prime}}_{lsq,\delta\phi}

\displaystyle=\Xi^{ij}_{knt,\eta\psi^{\prime}}(\Xi^{jkt,\eta\kappa^{\prime}}_{ls})^{*}G^{ijm,\alpha\beta}_{knt,(\eta\times f)(\psi^{\prime}\times f)}G^{jkt,(\eta\times f)(\kappa^{\prime}\times f)}_{lsq,\delta\phi}.

(411)

We obtain Eq.(
201
) from Eq.(
200
) by requiring Eq.(
206
) and
(\Omega^{mi}_{sqp,\epsilon^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}})^{*}=\Omega^{nm}_{qlp,\chi\epsilon^{\prime}}(\Omega^{ni}_{slp,\chi\gamma^{\prime}})^{*}
, where

\displaystyle(G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon‘}_{qps,\phi\gamma’})^{*}

\displaystyle=(\Omega^{mi}_{sqp,\epsilon^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}})^{*}(G^{ijm,\alpha(\epsilon‘\times f)}_{qps,\phi(\gamma^{\prime}\times f)})^{*},

\displaystyle G^{mkn,\beta\chi}_{lpq,\delta\epsilon^{\prime}}(G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa\gamma^{\prime}})^{*}

\displaystyle=\Omega^{nm}_{qlp,\chi\epsilon^{\prime}}(\Omega^{ni}_{slp,\chi\gamma^{\prime}})^{*}G^{mkn,\beta(\chi\times f)}_{lpq,\delta(\epsilon‘\times f)}(G^{itn,\psi(\chi\times f)}_{lps,\kappa(\gamma‘\times f)})^{*}.

(412)

We obtain Eq.(
202
) from Eq.(
197
) by requiring Eq.(
206
) and
(\Omega^{tj}_{qls,\kappa^{\prime}\phi^{\prime}})^{*}=(\Xi^{ij}_{qps,\phi^{\prime}\gamma})^{*}\Xi^{it}_{lps,\kappa^{\prime}\gamma}
, where

\displaystyle(G^{jkt,\eta\kappa^{\prime}}_{lsq,\delta\phi^{\prime}})^{*}

\displaystyle=(\Omega^{tj}_{qls,\kappa^{\prime}\phi^{\prime}})^{*}(G^{jkt,\eta(\kappa^{\prime}\times f)}_{lsq,\delta(\phi^{\prime}\times f)})^{*},

\displaystyle(G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,\phi^{\prime}\gamma})^{*}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,\kappa^{\prime}\gamma}

\displaystyle=(\Xi^{ij}_{qps,\phi^{\prime}\gamma})^{*}\Xi^{it}_{lps,\kappa^{\prime}\gamma}(G^{ijm,\alpha\epsilon}_{qps,(\phi^{\prime}\times f)(\gamma\times f)})^{*}G^{itn,\psi\chi}_{lps,(\kappa^{\prime}\times f)(\gamma\times f)}.

(413)
